# The wordpress.org rulebook

Every written rule and automated check a plugin submission is judged against, fetched from the official sources on 2026-10-04 (wordpress.org review 2). Part A is the published rules: the 18 guidelines, common issues, readme rules, the developer FAQ, developer pages, the review handbook and team posts. Part B is the automated checks: Plugin Check checks and sniffs, the pre-review issue titles, and the security and REST permission docs. Rows are quoted from the source, never paraphrased. The audit of the archive against these rows is in wordpress-org-submission.md.

## Part A -- wordpress.org plugin-directory rulebook — part A (written sources)

Built 2026-10-04 from the verbatim page saves in this folder; nothing was fetched. Each rule is a quote from its source, shortened only by cutting (marked …), and every quote is machine-checked against the saved file (verify-quotes.js): all match except HB-9, HB-204 (the save inlines glossary tooltip text such as "pluginPlugin A plugin is…", cut from the quote) and HB-156 (the save has the HTML entity "&#x5B;" for "["). The saved files do not state their own URLs, so each section gives the canonical page and marks it as not stated in the file.

ID scheme: G<n>.<k> = Detailed Plugin Guidelines (G0 = Developer Expectations preamble) · CI-<slug> = Common issues · RM-n = readme pages · FAQ-n = Plugin Developer FAQ · DEV-n = other developer sub-pages · HB-n = Plugin Review Team handbook · POST-n = Make/Plugins team posts.

Not covered here (Plugin Check internals, part B): checks-extract.txt, sniffs-extract.txt, extract.js, pc/. Ignored as instructed: rulebook-B.md, permission-sweep.md, arch/, fetchtext.mjs.

Total rows: 1039

## guidelines.txt — Detailed Plugin Guidelines

URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/detailed-plugin-guidelines/ (file says "Last updated March 11, 2026"; body "Last Updated: March 15, 2024"). G0.k = the "Developer Expectations" preamble, which binds like the numbered guidelines.

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| G0.1 | "Developers, all users with commit access, and all users who officially support a plugin are expected to abide by the following guidelines: Plugin Directory Guidelines … Community Guidelines … Forums Guidelines (should they use the forums/reviews)" | Process — not in code; check: every committer/support account on the plugin's Advanced page is a person bound by these | Developer Expectations |
| G0.2 | "Violations may result in plugins or plugin data … being removed from the directory until the issues are resolved. … Repeat violations may result in all the author's plugins being removed and the developer being banned" | Consequence clause — no grep; read every other G row as closure-grade | Developer Expectations |
| G0.3 | "It is the responsibility of the plugin developer to ensure their contact information on WordPress.org is up to date and accurate, in order that they receive all notifications from the plugins team." | Process — check: wordpress.org profile email of every owner/committer is a monitored human inbox | Developer Expectations |
| G0.4 | "Auto-replies and emails that route to a support system are not permitted" | Process — check: profile email is not a helpdesk address (Zendesk/Freshdesk/HelpScout) and sends no auto-reply | Developer Expectations |
| G0.5 | "All code in the directory should be made as secure as possible. Security is the ultimate responsibility of the plugin developer" | Full security pass: `$_POST`/`$_GET`/`$_REQUEST` without `sanitize_`; `echo` without `esc_`; `$wpdb->query(` without `prepare`; `register_rest_route` without real `permission_callback`; `wp_ajax_` handlers without `check_ajax_referer`+`current_user_can` | Developer Expectations |
| G0.6 | "Should a plugin be found to have security issues, it will be closed until the situation is resolved. In extreme cases the plugin may be updated by the WordPress Security team" | Same security pass as G0.5; a single exploitable endpoint = closure | Developer Expectations |
| G0.7 | "If you are uncertain whether a plugin might violate the guidelines, please contact `plugins@wordpress.org` and ask." | Process — check: borderline features (service gating, installers, remote code) asked about before upload | Developer Expectations |
| G1.1 | "Although any GPL-compatible license is acceptable, using the same license as WordPress — "GPLv2 or later" — is strongly recommended." | Main file header `License:` / `License URI:`; readme `License:` = `GPLv2 or later` | 1. GPL |
| G1.2 | "All code, data, and images — anything stored in the plugin directory hosted on WordPress.org — must comply with the GPL or a GPL-Compatible license." | Every file in the zip incl. `*.png`, `*.svg`, `*.woff*`, `*.json` data; `LICENSE*` files; icon/font packs (Font Awesome Pro, commercial icon sets) | 1. GPL |
| G1.3 | "Included third-party libraries, code, images, or otherwise, must be compatible." | `vendor/*/composer.json` `"license"`, `node_modules`/bundled JS header comments, `package.json` `license`; flag proprietary, CC-BY-NC, "all rights reserved" | 1. GPL |
| G2.1 | "It is the sole responsibility of plugin developers to ensure all files within their plugins comply with the guidelines." | Whole zip, incl. `vendor/`, build output and assets — not just own PHP | 2. Developer responsibility |
| G2.2 | "Intentionally writing code to circumvent guidelines, or restoring code they were asked to remove, is prohibited" | Diff against prior review emails: removed code returning behind a `define(`/filter/option flag, renamed function, or remote toggle | 2. Developer responsibility |
| G2.3 | "Developers are expected to confirm, before uploading to SVN, the licensing of all included files, from original source code to images and libraries." | Inventory of third-party files with licence of each (`vendor/`, `assets/`, `lib/`, `fonts/`) | 2. Developer responsibility |
| G2.4 | "they must comply to the terms of use for all third party services and APIs utilized by their plugins." | List every host hit by `wp_remote_get(`/`wp_remote_post(`/`wp_remote_request(`/`fetch(`; confirm each API's ToS permits the use (e.g. scraping, caching, commercial) | 2. Developer responsibility |
| G2.5 | "If there is no way to validate the licensing of a library or the terms of an API, then they cannot be used." | Any library without a `LICENSE`/header licence; any API host without public ToS → remove | 2. Developer responsibility |
| G3.1 | "The only version of the plugin that WordPress.org distributes is the one in the directory." | SVN `/tags/<Stable tag>/` = the shipped build; readme `Stable tag` | 3. Stable version available |
| G3.2 | "Distributing code via alternate methods, while not keeping the code hosted here up to date, may result in a plugin being removed." | Compare GitHub/own-site release version vs SVN `Version:`; self-update code (`Update URI:`, `pre_set_site_transient_update_plugins`) | 3. Stable version available |
| G4.1 | "Obscuring code by hiding it with techniques or systems similar to `p,a,c,k,e,r`'s obfuscate feature … is not permitted in the directory." | `eval(function(p,a,c,k,e`; `eval(`, `base64_decode(`, `gzinflate(`, `str_rot13(`, `\x[0-9a-f]{2}` runs, `chr(` chains | 4. Human readable |
| G4.2 | "… uglify's mangle … is not permitted in the directory." | `*.min.js` / single-line bundles with 1-letter identifiers and no matching unminified source in zip or readme link | 4. Human readable |
| G4.3 | "… unclear naming conventions such as `$z12sdf813d`, is not permitted in the directory." | Regex on PHP/JS identifiers like `\$[a-z_]{1,3}[0-9][a-z0-9]{5,}`; ionCube/SourceGuardian loaders (`sg_load(`, `ioncube`) | 4. Human readable |
| G4.4 | "We require developers to provide public, maintained access to their source code and any build tools in one of the following ways: Include the source code in the deployed plugin" | `src/` (or `assets/src`) shipped next to `build/`/`dist/`; `package.json`, `webpack.config.js`, `vite.config.*` present | 4. Human readable |
| G4.5 | "A link in the readme to the development location" | readme.txt contains a public repo URL (GitHub/GitLab) for the unminified source | 4. Human readable |
| G4.6 | "We strongly recommend you document how any development tools are to be used." | readme or `README.md` build steps (`npm install`, `npm run build`, `composer install`) | 4. Human readable |
| G5.1 | "Plugins may not contain functionality that is restricted or locked, only to be made available by payment or upgrade." | `is_pro`, `is_premium`, `license_key`, `license_status`, `if ( ! $licensed`, `disabled` UI with "Upgrade"/"Pro"/"Unlock"; code present but gated | 5. Trialware |
| G5.2 | "Functionality may not be disabled after a trial period or quota is met." | `trial`, `days_left`, `expires`, `install_date` + `time()` comparisons; local usage counters (`_count`, `_quota`, `_limit`) that disable local features | 5. Trialware |
| G5.3 | "plugins that provide sandbox only access to APIs and services are also trial, or test, plugins and not permitted." | API base URLs containing `sandbox`/`test` with no live endpoint; `sandbox` mode hard-coded | 5. Trialware |
| G5.4 | "Paid functionality in services is permitted (see guideline 6: serviceware), provided all the code inside a plugin is fully available." | Locked features must live in the remote service; any local code path gated on paid status (credits/licence) is a G5.1 hit | 5. Trialware |
| G5.5 | "We recommend the use of add-on plugins, hosted outside of WordPress.org, in order to exclude the premium code." | Premium code must not be in the zip: grep `pro/`, `premium/`, `class-*-pro*.php`, `if ( defined( '*_PRO' )` dead branches | 5. Trialware |
| G5.6 | "Situations where a plugin is intended as a developer tool only will be reviewed on a case by case basis." | Readme states developer-tool purpose if any gating exists | 5. Trialware |
| G5.7 | "Attempting to upsell the user on ad-hoc products and features is acceptable, provided it falls within bounds of guideline 11 (hijacking the admin experience)." | Upsell strings/links in `admin_notices`, settings pages, `plugin_action_links_` — check against G11 rows | 5. Trialware |
| G6.1 | "Plugins that act as an interface to some external third party service (e.g. a video hosting site) are allowed, even for paid services." | `wp_remote_` calls to the vendor's API; account/API-key settings field | 6. SaaS |
| G6.2 | "The service itself must provide functionality of substance" | Read what the remote endpoint does: real processing (AI, storage, delivery) vs. returning a yes/no or a value the plugin could compute | 6. SaaS |
| G6.3 | "… and be clearly documented in the readme file submitted with the plugin, preferably with a link to the service's Terms of Use." | readme `== External services ==` (or similar) naming service, data sent, when, links to ToS + privacy | 6. SaaS |
| G6.4 | "A service that exists for the sole purpose of validating licenses or keys while all functional aspects of the plugin are included locally is not permitted." | `wp_remote_post(` to `/license`, `/activate`, `/validate`, `/verify`; result stored in option then used to unlock local code | 6. SaaS |
| G6.5 | "Creation of a service by moving arbitrary code out of the plugin so that the service may falsely appear to provide supplemented functionality is prohibited." | Remote calls that return config, HTML, CSS or trivial transforms the plugin then renders; server-side "feature flags" | 6. SaaS |
| G6.6 | "Storefronts that are not services. A plugin that acts only as a front-end for products to be purchased from external systems will not be accepted." | Plugin whose only UI is a product/checkout list linking out; read main admin page purpose | 6. SaaS |
| G7.1 | "plugins may not contact external servers without explicit and authorized consent." | `wp_remote_`/`fetch(`/`file_get_contents('http` on `register_activation_hook`, `admin_init`, `init`, `wp_schedule_event` callbacks before an opt-in option is true | 7. No tracking |
| G7.2 | "This is commonly done via an 'opt in' method, requiring registration with a service or a checkbox within the plugin settings." | Opt-in option default `false`/absent in `add_option(`/`get_option( 'x', false )`; checkbox unchecked by default | 7. No tracking |
| G7.3 | "Documentation on how any user data is collected, and used, should be included in the plugin's readme, preferably with a clearly stated privacy policy." | readme section on data sent + privacy policy link; `wp_add_privacy_policy_content(` | 7. No tracking |

No tracking |

| G7.7 | "Undocumented (or poorly documented) use of external data (such as blocklists)." | Remote lists fetched (`wp_remote_get(` of `.txt`/`.json` lists, IP/spam/blocklists) not named in readme | 7. No tracking |

| G7.9 | "An exception to this policy is Software as a Service … By installing, activating, registering, and configuring plugins that utilize those services, consent is granted for those systems." | Calls happen only after the user configured the service (API key/account connected); readme documents it | 7. No tracking |

No remote executable code |

No remote executable code |
| G8.6 | "Using third party services to manage regularly updated lists of data, when not explicitly permitted in the service's terms of use" | Scheduled (`wp_schedule_event`) fetches of lists/datasets; check source ToS | 8. No remote executable code |
| G8.7 | "Using iframes to connect admin pages; APIs should be used to minimize security risks" | `<iframe` in admin page callbacks (`add_menu_page`/`add_submenu_page` renderers) pointing at an external app | 8. No remote executable code |
| G8.8 | "Management services that interact with and push software down to a site are permitted, provided the service handles the interaction on it's own domain and not within the WordPress dashboard." | Remote-management endpoints (`register_rest_route` that installs/updates) must be driven from the service's site, not a dashboard UI that pulls code | 8. No remote executable code |
| G9.1 | "the intent is to prevent plugins, developers, and companies from abusing the freedoms and rights of end users as well as other plugin developers." | Umbrella — read rows G9.2–G9.15 | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.2 | "Artificially manipulating search results via keyword stuffing, black hat SEO, or otherwise" | readme `Tags:`, title, short description and Description: repeated keywords; hidden links output on front end | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.3 | "Offering to drive more traffic to sites that use the plugin" | Copy promising backlinks/traffic/listing in a directory in exchange for use | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.4 | "Compensating, misleading, pressuring, extorting, or blackmailing others for reviews or support" | Review nags offering rewards/credits/discounts for a review; notices that block until rated; "rate 5 stars" prompts | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.5 | "Implying users must pay to unlock included features" | UI strings `Unlock`, `Pro`, `Premium`, lock icons on features whose code is in the zip | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.6 | "Creating accounts to generate fake reviews or support tickets (i.e. sockpuppeting)" | Process — not in code; check: no team/affiliated accounts reviewing the plugin | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.7 | "Taking other developers' plugins and presenting them as original work" | Forks: original copyright/author headers kept, original authors credited (readme `Contributors`/credits), licence preserved | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.8 | "Implying that a plugin can create, provide, automate, or guarantee legal compliance" | Plugin name, readme, UI strings: `GDPR compliant`, `CCPA`, `ADA compliant`, `WCAG compliant`, `compliance`, `legally` | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.9 | "Utilizing the user's server or resources without permission, such as part of a botnet or crypto-mining" | Miner scripts (`coinhive`, `cryptonight`, `.wasm` miners), outbound jobs in `wp_schedule_event` doing work for a third party, open proxies | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.10 | "Violations of the WordPress.org Community Code of Conduct" | Process — conduct; no code check | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.11 | "Violations of the WordCamp code of conduct" | Process — conduct; no code check | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.12 | "Violations of the Forum Guidelines" | Process — check: support-forum replies (no asking for admin logins, no moving support off-site against rules) | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.13 | "Harassment, threats, or abuse directed at any other member of the WordPress community" | Process — conduct; also UI/readme text attacking competitors | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.14 | "Falsifying personal information to intentionally disguise identities and avoid sanctions for previous infractions" | Process — accounts/ownership truthful | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G9.15 | "Intentionally attempting to exploit loopholes in the guidelines" | Read any construct that meets the letter but not the intent (e.g. "service" that only returns a licence flag; opt-in pre-ticked) | 9. Illegal/dishonest |
| G10.1 | "All "Powered By" or credit displays and links included in the plugin code must be optional and default to not show on users' front-facing websites." | `wp_footer`, `the_content`, `wp_head` output and shortcode/block render strings containing `Powered by`/author link; option default must be off | 10. No front-end credits |

| G11.7 | "Abusing the guidelines of an advertising system will result in developers being reported upstream." | Ad network code in admin (`adsbygoogle`) | 11. No admin hijack |
| G11.8 | "Developers are welcome and encouraged to include links to their own sites or social networks, as well as locally (within the plugin) including images to enhance that experience." | Allowed — but images must be local files, not `<img src="https://own-domain` | 11. No admin hijack |

| G18.3 | "… to grant exceptions and allow developers time to address issues, even security related." | Process — respond to team emails within given window | 18. Directory maintenance |
| G18.4 | "… to remove developer access to a plugin in lieu of a new, active, developer." | Process — keep plugin maintained (`Tested up to:` current, support answered) | 18. Directory maintenance |
| G18.5 | "… to make changes to a plugin, without developer consent, in the interest of public safety." | Process — check SVN for team-made security commits before next release | 18. Directory maintenance |

## common-issues.txt — Common issues

URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/common-issues/ (file: first published August 3, 2024, last updated March 20, 2026). Excerpts of the Review Team's rejection emails; the file says it is not exhaustive.

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| CI-sanitize-input | "Data that is input (either by a user or automatically) must be sanitized as soon as possible." | `$_POST[`, `$_GET[`, `$_REQUEST[`, `$_FILES[`, `$_COOKIE[`, `$_SERVER[`, `php://input`, `$request->get_param(` not wrapped in `sanitize_*`/`absint`/`intval`/`wp_kses*` at first use | Security › Sanitize |
| CI-validate | "All data should be validated, no matter what. Even when you sanitize, remember that you don't want someone putting in 'dog' when the only valid values are numbers." | Allow-list checks: `in_array( $x, $allowed, true )`, `is_numeric`, `absint`, enum checks, REST `args` with `validate_callback`/`enum`/`type` | Security › Sanitize |
| CI-escape-output | "Data that is output must be escaped properly when it is echo'd, so it can't hijack admin screens." | `echo`/`print`/`printf`/`?>…<?php echo` of variables without `esc_html`/`esc_attr`/`esc_url`/`esc_js`/`wp_kses*` | Security › Sanitize |
| CI-context-functions | "You must use the most appropriate functions for the context. If you're sanitizing email, use sanitize_email() , if you're outputting HTML, use wp_kses_post() , and so on." | Mismatches: `sanitize_text_field` on email/URL/HTML/textarea (use `sanitize_email`, `esc_url_raw`/`sanitize_url`, `wp_kses_post`, `sanitize_textarea_field`); `esc_attr` in text nodes, `esc_html` in `href` | Security › Sanitize |
| CI-escape-not-sanitize | "escape functions cannot be used to sanitize. They serve different purposes." | `esc_html(`/`esc_attr(`/`esc_url(` applied to `$_POST`/`$_GET` values that are then saved (`update_option(`, `update_post_meta(`, `$wpdb->insert(`) | Security › Sanitize: Confusion |
| CI-sanitize-then-escape | "If you are trying to echo the variable, you have to first sanitize it and then escape it, as for example: `echo esc_html(sanitize_text_field($_POST['example']));`" | `echo` of any superglobal: needs both `sanitize_*` and `esc_*` | Security › Sanitize: Confusion |
| CI-filter-param | "When using functions like `filter_var`, `filter_var_array`, `filter_input` and/or `filter_input_array` you will need to set the FILTER parameter to any kind of filter that sanitizes the input ." | `filter_input(`/`filter_var(`/`filter_input_array(`/`filter_var_array(` calls missing a `FILTER_SANITIZE_*`/`FILTER_VALIDATE_*` argument | Security › Sanitize: filter functions |
| CI-filter-default | "Leaving the filter parameter empty, PHP by default will apply the filter "FILTER_DEFAULT" which is not sanitizing at all." | `FILTER_DEFAULT`, `FILTER_UNSAFE_RAW` in filter calls | Security › Sanitize: filter functions |
| CI-nonce-sanitize | "When checking a nonce using `wp_verify_nonce` you will need to sanitize the input using `wp_unslash` AND `sanitize_text_field`" | `wp_verify_nonce( $_POST[` / `wp_verify_nonce( $_REQUEST[` without `sanitize_text_field( wp_unslash(` | Security › Sanitize: Nonces |
| CI-whole-input | "We strongly recommend you never attempt to process the whole $_POST/$_REQUEST/$_GET stack." | `foreach ( $_POST`, `foreach ( $_REQUEST`, `array_map( …, $_POST )`, `wp_unslash( $_POST )` whole, `update_option( 'x', $_POST )`, `extract( $_POST` | Security › Processing the whole input |
| CI-escape-echoed-vars | "all variables that are echoed need to be escaped when they're echoed, so it can't hijack users or (worse) admin screens." | Every `echo $`/`echo $this->`/`print $` without `esc_*`/`wp_kses*` | Security › Escape |
| CI-escape-late | "we ask you escape all $-variables, options, and any sort of generated data when it is being echoed. That means you should not be escaping when you build a variable, but when you output it at the end." | Variables built with `esc_html(` then echoed raw later (`$html .= esc_html(…); … echo $html;`) — escape at the `echo` | Security › Escape |
| CI-escape-options | "This remains true of options you've saved to the database. Even if you've properly sanitized when you saved, the tools for sanitizing and escaping aren't interchangeable." | `echo get_option(`, `echo get_post_meta(`, `echo get_user_meta(`, `echo $settings[` without `esc_*` | Security › Escape |
| CI-return-not-echo-json | "sometimes a function is echoing when it should really be returning content instead. This is a common mistake when it comes to returning JSON encoded content." | `echo json_encode(`/`echo wp_json_encode(` in AJAX/REST handlers → `wp_send_json*(`/`return new WP_REST_Response(` | Security › Escape |
| CI-sanitize-json | "Returning (which is what you would do with an API) can be json encoded, though remember to sanitize when you save to that json object!" | Values placed in arrays later `wp_json_encode`d / returned from REST are sanitized first | Security › Escape |
| CI-escape-html-too | "There are a number of options to secure all types of content (html, email, etc). Yes, even HTML needs to be properly escaped." | Echoed HTML strings/variables → `wp_kses_post(`/`wp_kses(` | Security › Escape |
| CI-esc-url-raw | "the `esc_url_raw` function is not an escaping function, but a sanitizing function similar to `sanitize_url`. Specifically it is used to sanitize a URL for use in a database or a redirection." | `echo esc_url_raw(`, `esc_url_raw(` inside `href=`/`src=` output | Escape › esc_url_raw |
| CI-esc-url | "The appropriate function to escape a URL is `esc_url`." | `href="<?php echo $url` / `src=` without `esc_url(` | Escape › esc_url_raw |
| CI-escape-__ | "The function `__` retrieves the translation without escaping, please either: Use an alternative function that escapes the resulting value such as `esc_html__` or `esc_attr__`. Or wrap the `__` function with a proper escaping function" | `echo __(`, `echo _x(`, `echo _n(`, `printf( __(`, `. __( ` concatenated into echoed HTML | Escape › Use of __ |
| CI-escape-_e | "The functions `_e` and `_ex` output the translation without escaping … An alternative to `_e` would be `esc_html_e`, `esc_attr_e` or simply using `__` wrapped by an escaping function and inside an `echo`." | `_e(` | Escape › Use of _e and _ex |
| CI-escape-_ex | "An alternative to `_ex` would be using `_x` wrapped by an escaping function and inside an `echo`." | `_ex(` | Escape › Use of _e and _ex |
| CI-wp-json-encode | "When you need to echo a JSON, it's better to make use of the function `wp_json_encode`" | `json_encode(` in plugin code (esp. echoed or in `<script>` / `data-` attributes) | Escape › json_encode |
| CI-json-flags | "make sure you are not avoiding escaping with the options passed on the second parameter." | `wp_json_encode(`/`json_encode(` with `JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES`, `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE`, missing `JSON_HEX_TAG` when inlined in HTML/script | Escape › json_encode |
| CI-kses-html | "When escaping, there are cases where your plugin will need to output HTML. This can be done using the functions `wp_kses_post` or `wp_kses`." | HTML output paths use `wp_kses_post(` or `wp_kses( $html, $allowed )` with explicit allowed list | Escape › HTML |
| CI-esc-html-on-html | "A common mistake is to use `esc_html` to escape HTML. This function is not intended for that, it's intended to escape the output that will go inside an HTML tag" | `esc_html(` around variables that contain markup (`$html`, `$output`, rendered templates) | Escape › HTML |
| CI-sanitize-not-escape | "Sanitize functions cannot be used to escape. They serve different purposes." | `echo sanitize_text_field(`, `echo sanitize_*(`, `echo absint(` used as the output escaper (except ints) | Escape › Using sanitizing functions |
| CI-wp-handle-upload | "When plugins use `move_uploaded_file()`, they exclude their uploads from the built-in checks and balances with WordPress's functions. Instead of that, you should use the built in function: wp_handle_upload" | `move_uploaded_file(`, `copy( $_FILES`, `file_put_contents(` into uploads from `$_FILES` → `wp_handle_upload(`/`media_handle_upload(` | Files › Use the WordPress file uploader |
| CI-unfiltered-uploads | "ALLOW_UNFILTERED_UPLOADS is not allowed. … we should not use or allow the use of this constant in any kind of logic, not even in a conditional." | `ALLOW_UNFILTERED_UPLOADS` anywhere (define, `defined(`, docs); `unfiltered_upload` cap grants | Files › Unfiltered uploads |
| CI-upload-mimes | "If you need to add a specific file that is not in the list and that won't represent a security risk, you can do so using the upload_mimes filter." | `add_filter( 'upload_mimes'` / `wp_check_filetype_and_ext` — added types must not be executable/scriptable (`svg` without sanitizer, `php`, `phtml`, `html`, `js`, `exe`); allowed only where needed (cap/context-scoped) | Files › Unfiltered uploads |
| CI-remote-assets | "Offloading images, js, css, and other scripts to your servers or any remote service (like Google, MaxCDN, jQuery.com etc) is disallowed." | `wp_enqueue_script(`/`wp_enqueue_style(`/`wp_register_*(` with `http`; `<script src="http`, `<link href="http`, `<img src="http`, CSS `url(https://` | Files › Calling files remotely |
| CI-include-locally | "If the file you're calling isn't a part of WordPress Core, then you should include it -locally- in your plugin, not remotely. If the file IS included in WordPress core, please call that instead." | Remote copies of core assets (jQuery, dashicons, jQuery UI) → core handles; others → bundle in `assets/` | Files › Calling files remotely |
| CI-remote-service-exception | "An exception to this rule is if your plugin is performing a service. We will permit this on a case by case basis." | Remote assets only from the documented service's own domain, for the service | Files › Calling files remotely |
| CI-no-remote-jquery-css | "Offloading jquery CSS files to Google – You should include the CSS in your plugin." | `ajax.googleapis.com/ajax/libs/jqueryui/…/themes/` style URLs | Files › Calling files remotely (not permitted) |
| CI-no-help-iframe | "Inserting an iframe with a help doc – A link, or including the docs in your plugin is preferred." | `<iframe` to docs/help sites in admin pages | Files › Calling files remotely (not permitted) |
| CI-no-own-domain-images | "Calling images from your own domain – They should be included in your plugin." | `<img src="https://<own-domain>`, `background-image: url(https://<own-domain>` in admin/front templates | Files › Calling files remotely (not permitted) |
| CI-ok-google-fonts | "Calling font families from Google or their approved CDN (if GPL compatible)" | `fonts.googleapis.com`/`fonts.gstatic.com` allowed; font licence GPL-compatible | Files › Calling files remotely (permitted) |
| CI-ok-service-api | "API calls back to your server to process possible spam comments (like Akismet)" | `wp_remote_post(` to own service doing real processing — must be documented (CI-3p-*) | Files › Calling files remotely (permitted) |
| CI-ok-offload-comments | "Offloading comments to your own servers (like Disqus)" | Embedded service scripts only when the plugin is that service's client | Files › Calling files remotely (permitted) |
| CI-ok-oembed | "oEmbed calls to a service provider (like Twitter or YouTube)" | `wp_oembed_get(`, `[embed]`, `WP_oEmbed` allowed | Files › Calling files remotely (permitted) |
| CI-service-readme | "If instead you feel you are providing a service, please re-write your readme.txt in a manner that explains the service, the servers being called, and if any account is needed to connect." | readme: service named, hostnames listed, account requirement stated | Files › Calling files remotely |
| CI-lib-stable | "We do not recommend you use the beta version of a library unless it has features required by your plugin. Instead, you should be using the most stable release of the library." | `composer.lock`/`package-lock.json`/bundled headers: versions containing `alpha`, `beta`, `rc`, `dev-`, `-dev` | Libraries › development versions |
| CI-lib-beta-explain | "If there is a technical reason you must use the beta version, please explain why. Otherwise, please change your library to the stable release." | Readme/reviewer reply explains any pre-release dependency | Libraries › development versions |
| CI-lib-unmaintained | "We no longer accept using any library that is no longer supported or maintained by their developers, as they pose a significant security risk." | `vendor/` + bundled JS: archived/abandoned packages (`"abandoned"` in `composer.lock`, archived GitHub repos, last release years old) | Libraries › Not maintained |
| CI-lib-outdated | "At least one of the 3rd party libraries you're using is out of date. Please upgrade to the latest stable version for better support and security." | Compare `composer.lock`/`package-lock.json`/bundled version banners with latest stable | Libraries › Out of Date |
| CI-wpdb-prepare | "You need to update your code to use wpdb calls and prepare() with your queries to protect them." | `$wpdb->query(`, `get_results(`, `get_row(`, `get_var(`, `get_col(` with `"…$var…"`/`. $var .` and no `$wpdb->prepare(`; `mysqli_`/`mysql_`/`PDO` direct use | WPDB: Unsafe SQL calls |
| CI-wpdb-array-placeholders | "You'll need to create a placeholder for each item of the array and pass all the corresponding values to those placeholders" | `IN ( " . implode(` / `IN ($ids)` built from arrays; should be `implode( ', ', array_fill( 0, count( $ids ), '%d' ) )` + prepare | WPDB: Arrays of placeholders |
| CI-no-heredoc | "Do not use HEREDOC or NOWDOC syntax in your plugins … we don't permit their use." | `<<<` in PHP files | Not use HEREDOC-NOWDOC |
| CI-direct-access | "You can easily prevent this by adding the following code at the top of all PHP files that could potentially execute code if accessed directly: `if ( ! defined( 'ABSPATH' ) ) exit;`" | Every PHP file with top-level executable code (function calls, `new`, `require`, hooks) starts with `defined( 'ABSPATH' )` / `defined( 'WPINC' )` guard | Direct file access |
| CI-prefix-unique | "All plugins must have unique function names, namespaces, defines, class and option names. This prevents your plugin from conflicting with other plugins or themes." | List all global `function `, `class `, `interface `, `trait `, `namespace `, `define(`/`const `, option/transient/meta keys, hook names, `global $` — all share one distinct prefix | Compatibility › Prefixing |
| CI-prefix-functions | "function ecpt_save_post()" | Top-level `function name(` outside a namespace without the plugin prefix | Compatibility › Prefixing (example) |
| CI-prefix-classes | "class ECPT_Admin{}" | Non-namespaced `class X` without prefix | Compatibility › Prefixing (example) |
| CI-prefix-namespace | "namespace ECPT;" | `namespace` first segment distinct to the plugin (not `App`, `Admin`, `Plugin`, `Core`) | Compatibility › Prefixing (example) |
| CI-prefix-options | "update_option( 'ecpt_settings', $settings );" | `add_option(`/`update_option(`/`get_option(`/`set_transient(`/`update_*_meta(` keys carry prefix | Compatibility › Prefixing (example) |
| CI-prefix-defines | "define( 'ECPT_LICENSE', true );" | `define( '` names carry prefix (not `VERSION`, `PLUGIN_DIR`, `DEBUG`) | Compatibility › Prefixing (example) |
| CI-prefix-globals | "global $ecpt_options;" | `global $` variable names carry prefix | Compatibility › Prefixing (example) |
| CI-prefix-length | "Don't try to use two (2) or three (3) letter prefixes anymore." | Prefix ≥ 4 characters | Compatibility › Prefixing |
| CI-prefix-reserved | "You also need to avoid the use of __ (double underscores), wp_ , or _ (single underscore) as a prefix. Those are reserved for WordPress itself." | Own functions/hooks/options starting `wp_`, `__`, `_`; also `WP_` classes and `wp_` option names | Compatibility › Prefixing |
| CI-function-exists | "using if (!function_exists('NAME')) { around all your functions and classes sounds like a great idea until you realize the fatal flaw. … Using if-exists should be reserved for shared libraries only." | `if ( ! function_exists(` / `if ( ! class_exists(` wrapping plugin-own definitions | Compatibility › Prefixing |
| CI-no-short-tags | "PHP: Do not use Short Tags … which is why we don't permit their use." | `<?=` and `<? ` (not `<?php`/`<?xml`) in PHP/templates | PHP › Short Tags |
| CI-no-global-ini | "Don't Force Set PHP Settings Globally … Having defines like ini_set('memory_limit', '-1'); run globally (like on init or in the __construct() part of your code) … may cause your users to fall out of compliance" | `ini_set(`, `set_time_limit(`, `ignore_user_abort(` at file scope, in `init`/`plugins_loaded`/`__construct` | PHP › Changing Settings globally |
| CI-ini-scoped | "If you must use those, you need to limit them specifically to only the exact functions that require them." | Remaining `ini_set(` only inside the specific long-running handler | PHP › Changing Settings globally |
| CI-no-default-timezone | "Setting a default timezone … is rarely a good idea. … WordPress explicitly sets and expects the default timezone to be UTC" | `date_default_timezone_set(`; prefer `wp_date(`, `current_time(`, `wp_timezone()` | PHP › Setting a default timezone |
| CI-no-error-reporting | "Don't Use Error Reporting in Production Code … It has no place in the day to day function of your plugin." | `error_reporting(`, `ini_set( 'display_errors'`, `@ini_set('display_errors'` | PHP › Error reporting |
| CI-main-file-name | "We expect the main plugin file (the file containing the plugin headers) to have the same name as the plugin folder, which is also the same name as the slug / permalink of the plugin." | File with `Plugin Name:` header = `<slug>/<slug>.php` | Plugin standards › Main file convention |
| CI-main-file-common-names | "Note that using some common names as the filename for the main plugin file can lead to issues in some configurations." | Main file named `plugin.php`, `index.php`, `main.php`, `init.php` | Plugin standards › Main file convention |
| CI-headers-complete | "Your headers are either missing or incomplete. Please review Header Requirements and update your plugin accordingly" | Main file docblock: `Plugin Name:`, `Version:`, `Description:`, `Author:`, `License:`, `Text Domain:`, `Requires at least:`, `Requires PHP:` (see DEV header rows) | Plugin standards › Incomplete Headers |
| CI-headers-main-only | "… putting the headers in only the main file." | `Plugin Name:` header present in exactly one PHP file in the zip (check `vendor/`, sub-plugins, examples) | Plugin standards › Incomplete Headers |
| CI-readme-complete | "In some cases, such as for first plugins, ones with dependencies, or plugins that call external services, we require you to provide a complete readme. This means your readme has to have headers as well as a proper description and documentation as to how it works and how one can use it." | readme.txt: full header block, `== Description ==`, usage/installation docs | Plugin standards › Incomplete Readme |
| CI-readme-no-surprises | "You are expected to provide users with all the information they need before they install your plugin." | readme states dependencies (`Requires Plugins`), accounts, external calls, costs | Plugin standards › Incomplete Readme |
| CI-readme-validate | "Your readme also must validate per Validator or we will reject it." | Run readme through wordpress.org readme validator; header fields parse | Plugin standards › Incomplete Readme |
| CI-readme-txt-not-md | "Keep in mind, we don't want to see a readme.MD. While they can work, a readme.txt file will always be given priority" | `readme.txt` exists at plugin root; `readme.md` alone is a fail | Plugin standards › Incomplete Readme |
| CI-readme-template | "We ask you please create your readme one based on this: https://wordpress.org/plugins/readme.txt" | Section names/order match the template (see RM rows) | Plugin standards › Incomplete Readme |
| CI-license-declared | "It is necessary to declare the license of this plugin. You can do this using the fields available both in the plugin readme and in the plugin headers." | readme `License:` + main file `License:` present | Plugin standards › No GPL-compatible license declared |
| CI-license-all-content | "all code, data, and images — anything stored in the plugin directory hosted on WordPress.org — must comply with the GPL or a GPL-Compatible license. Included third-party libraries, code, images, or otherwise, must be also compatible." | Third-party file licences (`vendor/`, fonts, icons, images) | Plugin standards › No GPL-compatible license declared |
| CI-stable-tag-match | "In your readme, your 'Stable Tag' does not match the Plugin Version as indicated in your main plugin file." | readme `Stable tag:` == main file `Version:` | Plugin standards › Incorrect Stable Tag |
| CI-stable-tag-not-wp | "Your Stable Tag is meant to be the stable version of your plugin, not of WordPress." | `Stable tag:` value is not a WP version (e.g. equals `Tested up to:`) | Plugin standards › Incorrect Stable Tag |
| CI-semver | "We recommend you use Semantic Versioning (aka SemVer) for managing versions" | `Version:` matches `^\d+\.\d+(\.\d+)?$` | Plugin standards › Incorrect Stable Tag |
| CI-no-stable-trunk | "While currently using the stable tag of trunk currently works in the Plugin Directory, it's not actually a supported or recommended method … and has been known to cause issues with automatic updates." | readme `Stable tag: trunk` | Plugin standards › Incorrect Stable Tag |
| CI-tag-and-increment | "We ask you please properly use tags and increment them when you release new versions of your plugin, just like you update the plugin version in the main file." | SVN `/tags/<Version>/` exists for each release; version increased | Plugin standards › Incorrect Stable Tag |
| CI-license-same | "Please make sure that you are declaring the same license in both the readme file and the plugin headers." | readme `License:` string == main file `License:` string | Plugin standards › Declared license mismatched |
| CI-license-sole | "It is fine for this plugin to contain code from other sources under other licenses as long those are well documented and are under GPL or GPL-Compatible license, but we need a sole license declared for your code." | One licence in headers; third-party licences documented (readme credits / `LICENSE` per library) | Plugin standards › Declared license mismatched |
| CI-http-api | "WordPress comes with an extensive HTTP API that should be used instead of creating your own curl calls." | `curl_init(`, `curl_exec(`, `curl_setopt(`, `fsockopen(`, `file_get_contents('http`, `fopen('http` in own code → `wp_remote_get(`/`wp_remote_post(`/`wp_safe_remote_*(` | Use HTTP API |
| CI-http-api-curl-hook | "In case you need, you can use setopt with https://developer.wordpress.org/reference/hooks/http_api_curl/" | Custom curl options via `add_action( 'http_api_curl'` instead of raw curl | Use HTTP API |
| CI-http-api-vendor-ok | "If you're using CURL in 3rd party vendor libraries, that's permitted. It's in your own code unique to this plugin (or any dedicated WordPress libraries) that we need it corrected." | Separate `curl_` hits: `vendor/` (ok) vs own code (fix) | Use HTTP API |
| CI-enqueue-js | "When including JavaScript code you can use: wp_register_script() and wp_enqueue_script() to add JavaScript code from a file. wp_add_inline_script() to add inline JavaScript code to previous declared scripts." | Raw `<script` tags echoed in PHP (`echo '<script`, `?><script`), `wp_head`/`wp_footer`/`admin_footer` printing scripts | Use wp_enqueue commands |
| CI-enqueue-css | "When including CSS you can use: wp_register_style() and wp_enqueue_style() to add CSS from a file. wp_add_inline_style() to add inline CSS to previously declared CSS." | Raw `<style`/`<link rel="stylesheet"` echoed in PHP | Use wp_enqueue commands |
| CI-enqueue-attributes | "Note that as of WordPress 5.7, you can pass attributes like async, nonce, and type by using new functions and filters" | Script attributes via `wp_script_attributes`/`wp_inline_script_attributes` filters, `wp_print_script_tag(`, `strategy` arg — not hand-written tags | Use wp_enqueue commands |
| CI-enqueue-admin | "If you're trying to enqueue on the admin pages you'll want to use the admin enqueues. admin_enqueue_scripts admin_print_scripts admin_print_styles" | Admin assets hooked on `admin_enqueue_scripts` (ideally `$hook_suffix`-scoped), not `init`/`admin_head` echo | Use wp_enqueue commands |
| CI-core-libs | "Your plugin has included a copy of a library that WordPress already includes. … plugins may not include those libraries in their own code, but instead must use the versions of those libraries packaged with WordPress." | Bundled jQuery, jQuery UI, SimplePie, PHPMailer, PHPass, Underscore, Backbone, Moment, Lodash, React (see G13) | Including Libraries Already In Core |
| CI-core-libs-addons-only | "It's fine to locally include add-ons not in core, but please ONLY add those additional files. For example, you do not need the entire jQuery UI library for one file." | Full `jquery-ui` bundles where only one widget is used → enqueue core `jquery-ui-<widget>` handle | Including Libraries Already In Core |
| CI-core-libs-noconflict | "If your code doesn't work with the built-in versions of jQuery, it's most likely a noConflict issue." | JS using `$(` at top level instead of `jQuery(function($){…})` | Including Libraries Already In Core |
| CI-i18n-text-literal | "please do not use variables or function calls for the text … parameters of any gettext function, all of them NEED to be strings." | `__( $`, `esc_html__( $`, `_e( $`, `_n( $`, `__( sprintf(` / constants as text arg | Internationalization › Using variables |
| CI-i18n-context-literal | "please do not use variables or function calls for the … context … parameters of any gettext function" | `_x( 'x', $ctx`, `esc_html_x( 'x', $`, `_ex(`/`_nx(` with variable context | Internationalization › Using variables |
| CI-i18n-domain-literal | "This also applies to the translation domain, this is a bad call: `esc_html__( 'Hello, how are you?' , $plugin_slug );`" | Text domain arg as variable/constant (`$this->domain`, `PREFIX_TEXT_DOMAIN`) | Internationalization › Using variables |
| CI-i18n-domain-slug | "Also note that the translation domain needs to be the same as your plugin slug." | All gettext domains == slug == header `Text Domain:` | Internationalization › Using variables |
| CI-i18n-placeholders | "you need to add a placeholder which will be part of the string and change it after the gettext function does its magic, you can use `printf` to do so" | Strings concatenated with variables inside gettext; `printf`/`sprintf` with placeholders and a `/* translators: */` comment | Internationalization › Using variables |
| CI-active-plugins | "It is not allowed for plugins to change the activation status of other plugins, this is an action that must be performed by the user." | `activate_plugin(`, `activate_plugins(`, `deactivate_plugins(` on other plugins' basenames; `update_option( 'active_plugins'`; `update_site_option( 'active_sitewide_plugins'` | Compliance › Changing Active Plugins |
| CI-activation-interfere | "It is also not allowed to interfere with the user's actions when activating or deactivating a plugin, unless that's done to prevent errors" | Hooks on `activate_plugin`/`deactivate_plugin`/`activated_plugin`/`deactivated_plugin`, `plugin_action_links` removing Deactivate, filters on `option_active_plugins`/`pre_update_option_active_plugins`, deactivation interstitials blocking the action | Compliance › Changing Active Plugins |
| CI-self-deactivate-ok | "(For example: When your plugin depends on another plugin, deactivate your own plugin when that other plugin is not active)." | Only `deactivate_plugins( plugin_basename( __FILE__ ) )` on missing dependency is allowed | Compliance › Changing Active Plugins |
| CI-plugin-dependencies | "WordPress 6.5 introduces Plugin Dependencies , you can use it to manage dependencies (although it's fine if you use this as a fallback)." | Main file header `Requires Plugins:` with wordpress.org slugs | Compliance › Changing Active Plugins |
| CI-no-update-checker | "Please remove the checks you have in your plugin to provide for updates. We do not permit plugins to phone home to other servers for updates" | `pre_set_site_transient_update_plugins`, `site_transient_update_plugins`, `plugins_api`, `update_plugins_{hostname}`, `Puc_v*`/`PucFactory`/`plugin-update-checker`, EDD `EDD_SL_Plugin_Updater`, header `Update URI:` | Compliance › Update checker |
| CI-no-updater-interference | "We also ask that plugins not interfere with the built-in updater as it will cause users to have unexpected results with WordPress 5.5 and up." | `auto_update_plugin` filter forcing values, `remove_action( … 'wp_update_plugins'`, `automatic_updater_disabled`, `DISALLOW_FILE_MODS` toggling, hiding update rows | Compliance › Update checker |
| CI-3p-disclose | "We require plugins that reach out to other services to disclose this, in clear and plain language, so users are aware of where data is being sent." | Every external host from `wp_remote_*`/`fetch(`/`wp_enqueue_*` URLs/iframes appears in readme `== External services ==` | Compliance › Undocumented 3rd party |
| CI-3p-even-own | "This is true even if you are the 3rd party service." | Own SaaS/API domain documented like any third party | Compliance › Undocumented 3rd party |
| CI-3p-explain | "Clearly explain that your plugin is relying on a 3rd party as a service and under what circumstances" | readme per service: what it is, what data is sent, when (trigger) | Compliance › Undocumented 3rd party |
| CI-3p-link-service | "Provide a link to the service." | readme per service: service URL | Compliance › Undocumented 3rd party |
| CI-3p-link-terms | "Provide a link to the service terms of use and/or privacy policies." | readme per service: ToS URL + privacy policy URL (both resolve) | Compliance › Undocumented 3rd party |
| CI-unneeded-devtools | "This plugin includes folders and files that looks like are not required for the running of your plugin. Some examples are: development tools" | Zip contains `.github/`, `.vscode/`, `.idea/`, `bin/`, `tools/`, `Gruntfile.js`, `gulpfile.js`, `phpcs.xml`, `.editorconfig`, `.eslintrc` | Included Unneeded Folders |
| CI-unneeded-vendor | "unneeded vendor folders for production (bower, node, grunt, etc)" | `node_modules/`, `bower_components/`, dev-only `vendor/` packages (`phpunit`, `squizlabs`, `wp-coding-standards`) | Included Unneeded Folders |
| CI-unneeded-demos | "demos" | `demo/`, `examples/`, `sample/` folders | Included Unneeded Folders |
| CI-unneeded-tests | "unit tests" | `tests/`, `phpunit.xml*`, `*.spec.js`, `*.test.js`, `e2e/` in the zip | Included Unneeded Folders |
| CI-keep-composer-json | "You should also keep and/or link configuration files, as for example, the `composer.json` file in order to allow others to review, study, and yes, fork this code." | `composer.json` (and `package.json`) shipped or linked when built code is present | Included Unneeded Folders |
| CI-not-permitted-files | "We have detected files that are not among of the files normally found in a plugin, are they necessary? If not, then those won't be allowed." | Allowed set: php, js, css, txt, md, png, svg, jpg, json, xml. Flag `.exe`, `.dll`, `.so`, `.sh`, `.bat`, `.phar`, `.zip`, `.tar*`, `.sql`, `.log`, `.env`, `.git/`, `.DS_Store`, `.htaccess` | Not permitted files |
| CI-premium-source | "Some premium libraries are specifically not permitted to be included in free (WordPress.org hosted) plugins. Those must be removed." | Commercial libs (e.g. Font Awesome Pro, ACF Pro, Slider Revolution, licensed fonts) in `vendor/`/`assets/` | Not permitted files › Including code from a "premium" source |
| CI-gpl-non-compatible | "It's required that all code be compatible with the GPLv2 (or later) license in order to be included in our directory. You must remove the code and alter the plugin so it is not required." | Licence headers of each bundled file; non-GPL (proprietary, CC-BY-NC, JSON licence, SSPL) | GPL › Non-GPL compatible Code Included |
| CI-gpl-source-for-compressed | "we require you to include the source code and/or a link to the non-compressed, developer libraries you've included in your plugin." | For each `*.min.js`/`*.min.css`/`build/` bundle: unminified source in zip or public repo link | GPL › No publicly documented resource |
| CI-gpl-source-link-in-readme | "If you include a link, this may be in your source code, however we require you to also have it in your readme." | readme.txt contains the source repo URL (not only a code comment) | GPL › No publicly documented resource |
| CI-gpl-build-public | "we require plugins to make the source code to any compressed files available to the public in an easy to find location, by documenting it in the readme." | readme section naming the repo and where the source of compiled files lives | GPL › No publicly documented resource |
| CI-gpl-build-directions | "We strongly recommend you include directions on the use of any build tools to encourage future developers." | readme/`README.md` build instructions (`npm run build`, `composer install`) | GPL › No publicly documented resource |
| CI-composer-json | "we would like to ask you to include that file in your plugin, even if it is only used for development purposes." | `vendor/autoload.php` present ⇒ `composer.json` present in zip root | GPL › Using composer but no composer.json file |

## how-your-readme-txt-works.txt — Plugin Readmes

URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/how-your-readme-txt-works/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| RM-1 | "each plugin should have a readme file named `readme.txt` that adheres to the WordPress plugin readme file standard ." | `readme.txt` (lowercase) at plugin root | Plugin Readmes intro |
| RM-2 | "You can use the plugin readme generator and put your completed result through the official readme validator to check it." | Run readme validator; zero errors/warnings | Plugin Readmes intro |
| RM-3 | "Since WordPress 5.8 plugin readme files are not parsed for requirements . This means that headers `Requires PHP` and `Requires at least` are going to be parsed from plugin's main PHP file." | Main file header has `Requires at least:` and `Requires PHP:`; values agree with readme | Plugin Readmes intro |
| RM-4 | "The `readme.txt` file is intended to be written using a subset of markdown." | readme uses `=== Name ===`, `== Section ==`, `= Sub =`; no HTML blocks | Section Details |
| RM-5 | "Contributors – a case sensitive, comma separated list of all WordPress.org usernames who have contributed to the code." | readme `Contributors:` — exact wordpress.org usernames, comma separated, case matches profile | Readme Header Information |
| RM-6 | "It is generally considered respectful to include the names of people who worked on forked projects. Some developers will ask to be removed from the list … It's best to honor those requests." | Forks: original authors listed unless they asked to be removed | Readme Header Information |
| RM-7 | "Remember to only use the WordPress.org username – anything else will show up sans profile link and gravatar." | `Contributors:` entries are not display names/emails/company names | Readme Header Information |
| RM-8 | "Donate link – (OPTIONAL) Makes a "Donate to this plugin" link in the sidebar." | `Donate link:` optional; if present a valid URL | Readme Header Information |
| RM-9 | "Tags – 1 to 5 comma separated terms that describe the plugin." | `Tags:` has 1–5 entries | Readme Header Information |
| RM-10 | "Plugins must refrain from using competitors plugin names as tags." | `Tags:` contain no competitor plugin names | Readme Header Information |
| RM-11 | "Plugins should not use tags which are unique to that plugin, as these will not be shown." | `Tags:` not the plugin's own brand name | Readme Header Information |
| RM-12 | "Tested up to – The version of WordPress that the plugin has been tested against. … a plugin only need to define the major version it is tested against" | `Tested up to:` = current major WP (e.g. `6.9`), no minor needed | Readme Header Information |
| RM-13 | "This should be numbers only, so '4.9' and not 'WP 4.9'" | `Tested up to:` matches `^\d+\.\d+$` | Readme Header Information |
| RM-14 | "Requires PHP – (OPTIONAL) The required minimum version of PHP needed for use with this plugin. This should be numbers only, so '7.0' and not 'PHP 7.0'" | `Requires PHP:` numeric only | Readme Header Information |
| RM-15 | "Stable Tag – The stable version of the plugin. This is not the version of WordPress, but the version of the plugin itself." | `Stable tag:` == main file `Version:` | Readme Header Information |
| RM-16 | "Only use numbers and periods, and SemVer formatting is recommended." | `Stable tag:` matches `^[0-9.]+$` | Readme Header Information |
| RM-17 | "License – The GPLV2 (or later) compatible license used for the plugin." | readme `License:` GPL-compatible, equals header `License:` | Readme Header Information |
| RM-18 | "License URI – (OPTIONAL) A link to the license. This is optional, but if a plugin uses a more rare license, strongly recommended." | `License URI:` present when licence is not GPLv2+ | Readme Header Information |
| RM-19 | "At the end of the header section is a place for a short description of a plugin. The example recommends no more than 150 characters and to not use markup." | Line after header block: ≤150 chars, no markdown/HTML | Readme Header Information |
| RM-20 | "If it's longer than 150 characters, it gets cut off, so keep it short." | Count short description length | Readme Header Information |
| RM-21 | "If your plugin has no custom install settings, it's okay to omit this section. If your plugin has custom configuration notes post install, this is a great place to put that information." | `== Installation ==` present when setup (API key, account) is needed | Installation |
| RM-22 | "While custom sections are permitted and supported, please use them in moderation." | Count non-standard `== … ==` sections | Custom Sections |
| RM-23 | "When WordPress.org parses the `readme.txt`, the very first thing it does is to look at the `readme.txt` in the `/trunk` directory, where it reads the "Stable Tag" line." | SVN `trunk/readme.txt` `Stable tag:` points at an existing tag | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-24 | "So a Stable Tag of "1.2.3" will make it look for `/tags/1.2.3/`." | SVN `/tags/<Stable tag>/` exists | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-25 | "The readme.txt in the tag folder must also be properly updated to have the correct "Stable Tag" — failing to do so may cause your plugin to not be updatable." | `tags/<v>/readme.txt` `Stable tag:` == `<v>` | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-26 | "If the Stable Tag is 1.2.3 and `/tags/1.2.3/` exists, then nothing in trunk will be read any further … Everything comes from the `readme.txt` in the file being pointed to by the Stable Tag." | Readme fixes (External services, Tested up to) must land in the tag folder, not only trunk | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-27 | "That version number comes from the plugin's main PHP file, not the readme!" | Main file `Version:` in the tagged folder equals tag name | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-28 | "If you have changed Stable Tag to 1.4 and the plugin still says 1.3 in the PHP file, then the version listed will be 1.3." | `tags/<v>/<slug>.php` `Version:` == `<v>` | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-29 | "We are currently actively discouraging the use of "Stable Tag: trunk" and prohibiting its use for new plugins." | readme `Stable tag: trunk` = fail for new plugins | How The Readme Is Parsed |
| RM-30 | "We recommend you NOT have the video as the final line in a FAQ section, as sometimes formatting gets weird." | Last line of `== Frequently Asked Questions ==` is not a bare video URL | Videos |
| RM-31 | "The Plugin URI should be unique to each plugin. Do not use the same URI for your free and pro plugin." | Header `Plugin URI:` differs from the Pro plugin's `Plugin URI:` | Field Details |
| RM-32 | "While readmes are simple text files, having a file larger than 10k may result in errors. Your readme should be brief and to the point." | `readme.txt` size ≤ 10 KB | File Size |
| RM-33 | "The description should not be a sales pitch as much as a description of the plugin, what it does, and how to use it." | `== Description ==` explains function/usage; flag marketing superlatives, pricing tables | File Size |
| RM-34 | "Your install directions should be direct. Your FAQ should actually address issues." | `== Installation ==` concise; FAQ entries are real questions | File Size |
| RM-35 | "As for your changelog, we recommend keeping the current release in the readme and splitting the rest out out into it's own file — `changelog.txt` for example." | `== Changelog ==` length; older entries in `changelog.txt` | File Size |
| RM-36 | "if you need in-depth documentation with inline images and so on, direct people to your own website." | No long docs/images in readme; link to docs site | File Size |

## readme-example.txt — readme.txt template

URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical https://wordpress.org/plugins/readme.txt (the template CI-readme-template points to)

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| RM-37 | "=== Plugin Name === Contributors: … Donate link: … Tags: … Requires at least: … Tested up to: … Stable tag: … Requires PHP: … License: … License URI: …" | readme header has these fields in this block, first line `=== Name ===` | readme template header |
| RM-38 | "Here is a short description of the plugin. This should be no more than 150 characters. No markup here." | Short description ≤150 chars, plain text | readme template header |
| RM-39 | "This is the long description. No limit, and you can use Markdown" | `== Description ==` present | Description |
| RM-40 | "For backwards compatibility, if this section is missing, the full length of the short description will be used" | `== Description ==` present (do not rely on fallback) | Description |
| RM-41 | ""Contributors" is a comma separated list of wordpress.org usernames" | `Contributors:` usernames only | Description notes |
| RM-42 | ""Tags" is a comma separated list of tags that apply to the plugin" | `Tags:` comma separated, relevant | Description notes |
| RM-43 | ""Requires at least" is the lowest version that the plugin will work on" | `Requires at least:` = real minimum WP (check functions used, e.g. `wp_admin_notice` 6.4, `Requires Plugins` 6.5) | Description notes |
| RM-44 | ""Tested up to" is the highest version that you've *successfully used to test the plugin*" | `Tested up to:` only a version actually tested | Description notes |
| RM-45 | "Stable tag must indicate the Subversion "tag" of the latest stable version" | `Stable tag:` = latest `/tags/<v>/` | Description notes |
| RM-46 | "If the `/trunk/readme.txt` file says that the stable tag is `4.3`, then it is `/tags/4.3/readme.txt` that'll be used for displaying information about the plugin." | Edit tag readme for listing changes | Description notes |
| RM-47 | "you can update the trunk `readme.txt` to reflect changes in your in-development version … as long as the trunk's `readme.txt` points to the correct stable tag." | trunk `Stable tag:` points to released tag | Description notes |
| RM-48 | "If no stable tag is provided, your users may not get the correct version of your code." | `Stable tag:` line present | Description notes |
| RM-49 | "1. This screen shot description corresponds to screenshot-1.(png\|jpg\|jpeg\|gif). Screenshots are stored in the /assets directory." | `== Screenshots ==` numbered captions ↔ SVN `assets/screenshot-N.png\|jpg\|jpeg\|gif` | Screenshots |
| RM-50 | "List versions from most recent at top to oldest at bottom." | `== Changelog ==` order descending; current version entry present | Changelog |
| RM-51 | "Upgrade notices describe the reason a user should upgrade. No more than 300 characters." | Each `== Upgrade Notice ==` entry ≤300 chars | Upgrade Notice |
| RM-52 | "Links require brackets and parenthesis" | Readme links as `[text](url)` | Markdown example |

## plugin-developer-faq.txt — Plugin Developer FAQ
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/plugin-developer-faq/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| FAQ-1 | "we reply to all emails within 7 business days" | Process — not in code; check: email responses to plugins@wordpress.org within SLA | FAQ › How do I contact the Plugins team? |
| FAQ-2 | "It's a .zip file and under 10Mb" | Verify `du -h plugin.zip` is under 10 MB; .zip archive format | FAQ › Where do I submit my plugin? |
| FAQ-3 | "It's in the common WordPress plugin format so that it can be installed using the 'Upload Plugin' feature in WordPress" | `readme.txt` at `/trunk/` root; main plugin file has `<?php` header with `Plugin Name:`, `Plugin URI:`, `Description:` | FAQ › Where do I submit my plugin? |
| FAQ-4 | "production-ready: complete, without errors, without unnecessary logs, without development tools, and that all necessary files are properly compiled or generated" | No `console.log`, `var_dump`, `error_log` calls; no `.map` files; no uncompiled SCSS/JSX; no test utilities | FAQ › Where do I submit my plugin? |
| FAQ-5 | "We do not accept placeholders or plugins that aren't ready to be used" | No `__` (TODO markers), `coming soon`, `in progress`, "v0.", "alpha", "beta" in readme/code | FAQ › Where do I submit my plugin? |
| FAQ-6 | "The majority of plugins who face this issue have included all sorts of development content that has no place in the final code" | No `/tests/`, `/vendor/` unpruned, `/node_modules/`, `/docs/`, `Makefile`, `.git/`, `.github/`, `.npmrc`, `webpack.config.js`, `babel.config.js`, `jest.config.js`, `phpunit.xml` | FAQ › What if my plugin is over 10 megs? |
| FAQ-7 | "If we don't find any issues related to security, compatibility, compliance with the guidelines, or overall presentation, your plugin will be approved" | Process — code review gates: security, compatibility, guideline compliance, presentation | FAQ › What happens after submission? |
| FAQ-8 | "If your plugin is small and all the code is correct, it should be approved within fourteen days of initial review" | Process — initial review SLA: ≤14 days if no issues detected | FAQ › How long does it take to get a plugin approved? |
| FAQ-9 | "We aim to reply to all reviews within ten (10) business days" | Process — review feedback response SLA: ≤10 business days | FAQ › I sent in the fixes but no one replied. How long should I wait? |
| FAQ-10 | "There's no timeline and as long as we know you're working on it and we feel you're making progress, we'll leave the review open. Your plugin will be rejected after 3 months" | Process — review rejection after 3 months of inactivity; team judges progress | FAQ › If my plugin has a problem, how long do I have to fix it? |
| FAQ-11 | "If your plugin review is not complete after three (3) months, we will reject your submission in order to keep the queue maintainable" | Process — auto-rejection SLA: 3 months from initial submission | FAQ › Why was my plugin rejected after three months? |
| FAQ-12 | "Generally, just one. If you're a plugin author with more than one million active plugin installations, we understand that you have more ongoing projects, so you would have a different limit of up to 10 plugin submissions at the same time" | Process — max concurrent submissions: 1 (or ≤10 if >1M active installations) | FAQ › How many plugins can I submit for review at a time? |
| FAQ-13 | "No. And if you do so, we will suspend all your secondary accounts" | Process — do not use multiple accounts to circumvent one-submission limit; all accounts suspended if violated | FAQ › Can I submit multiple plugins with multiple accounts? |
| FAQ-14 | "Not including a readme.txt file when acting as a service" | Every plugin must include `readme.txt`; required for directory listing | FAQ › Are there specific things that I should avoid doing? |
| FAQ-15 | "Not testing the plugin with WP_DEBUG" | Plugins must be tested with `WP_DEBUG true`; check: no warnings, errors on debug output | FAQ › Are there specific things that I should avoid doing? |
| FAQ-16 | "Including custom versions of packaged JavaScript libraries" | `wp_enqueue_script()` uses official CDN/npm versions only; no vendored fork of `jquery.js`, `moment.js`, `lodash.js`, etc. | FAQ › Are there specific things that I should avoid doing? |
| FAQ-17 | "Calling external files unnecessarily" | Minimize `wp_remote_get`, `wp_remote_post`, `file_get_contents('http` calls; justify each external call | FAQ › Are there specific things that I should avoid doing? |
| FAQ-18 | ""Powered By" links" | No `admin_footer_text` hook outputting author name/link; grep: `echo.*author`, `sprintf.*Company` in filters/hooks | FAQ › Are there specific things that I should avoid doing? |
| FAQ-19 | "Phoning home" | No `wp_remote_post( 'https://external.com', ['body' => ['site_url' => get_option('home'), 'admin_email' => get_option('admin_email')]] )` on activation/cron without default-off toggle; grep: `add_option()` telemetry flag | FAQ › Are there specific things that I should avoid doing? |
| FAQ-20 | "We do not accept plugins that have no meaningful purpose or provide no practical functionality" | Plugin `Description:` in readme and plugin header must describe concrete functionality; grep: no placeholder, "coming soon", "v0", "beta", "alpha" | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-21 | "We also do not accept plugins that facilitate illegal activity or encourage harmful behavior. This includes, but is not limited to, black-hat SEO tools, content spinners, fake content creators, scrappers, hate-related plugins, and similar software" | No `generate_content()`, `auto_post()`, `scrape_urls()`, `spin_text()` functions; grep: `curl_exec()` for scraping, content generation loops | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-22 | "We also do not accept new plugins that allow arbitrary code insertion or execution. Examples include PHP or JavaScript editors, file managers, and AI tools that generate code intended to be executed on the site. HTML output is permitted, provided it is properly escaped and handled securely" | No code execution endpoints; PHP/JS editor plugins blocked; AI code generation blocked unless HTML-only with escaping; grep: `eval(`, `exec(`, file edit UIs | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-23 | "Plugins must not download or execute code from external sources or serve just as a tool to install other plugins" | No remote code download/execute; grep: `wp_remote_*` for code, `system()`, `shell_exec()`, plugin installer UIs | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-24 | "Similarly we do not accept framework plugins or library plugins. If your plugin has to require other plugins or themes to edit themselves in order to use your plugin, it's a library. If your plugin is a template from which more code can be built by customizing the files directly, it's a framework or boilerplate" | Plugin header has singular `Plugin Name:` (not template); no `require_once()` chain to other plugins for activation; check: no `.template` variants or `Boilerplate` in readme | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-25 | "Frameworks and libraries should be packaged with each plugin (hopefully in a way that doesn't conflict with other plugins using the framework or libraries)" | Bundled code uses unique `namespace MyPlugin\Library\` or `class_prefix_` to avoid collisions; grep: no global class names like `Core`, `Helper` | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-26 | "We do not accept plugins that are essentially copies of existing work or that merely duplicate functionality already provided by WordPress Core" | Plugin code is original (not copy-pasted from Core or other directory plugin); grep: unique function signatures, no `wp_get_post_meta` reimplementations | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-27 | "Likewise, we generally do not accept plugins whose functionality is already extensively represented in the Plugin Directory by hundreds of comparable alternatives, unless they provide clear, meaningful differentiation or introduce substantial innovation" | Process — not in code; check: readme `== Description ==` states what sets the plugin apart from the many directory plugins doing the same job | FAQ › Are there plugins you don't accept? |
| FAQ-28 | "No, you should rewrite and upgrade the existing plugin. Make it a major version release" | Do not resubmit as new plugin; bump `Version:` to major (e.g., 2.0.0 or 3.0.0) in plugin header and tag | FAQ › I want to redo, upgrade, or rebrand my existing plugin. I just submit again, right? |
| FAQ-29 | "You can update your plugin files from the submission page at any time" | Process — plugin files editable via submission review form until approval | FAQ › I made a mistake with my submission. How can I fix it? |
| FAQ-30 | "You can update your slug once after submitting it" | Process — slug (permalink) changeable once post-submission; thereafter permanent | FAQ › I made a mistake with my submission. How can I fix it? |
| FAQ-31 | "Plugins may not use vulgarities in the name or slug" | `Plugin Name:` header and slug: grep for no profanity, no slurs in plugin-name.php or readme.txt first line | FAQ › Are there things I can't do in a plugin name? |
| FAQ-32 | "Plugins may not use 'WordPress' or 'Plugin' in their slugs except under extreme situations" | Slug and folder name: no `wordpress` and no `plugin` token (regex `(^\|-)(wordpress\|plugin)(-\|$)` on the slug) | FAQ › Are there things I can't do in a plugin name? |
| FAQ-33 | "Plugins may not use version numbers in plugin slugs" | Slug, folder name, `Plugin Name:` header and readme `=== Name ===`: no version tokens (regex `v?\d+(\.\d+)*`, e.g. `-2`, `-v3`, `1.0`) | FAQ › Are there things I can't do in a plugin name? |
| FAQ-34 | "Due to system limitations, only English letters and Arabic numbers are permitted in the slug" | Slug matches `^[a-z0-9-]+$` (English letters, Arabic numerals, hyphens only) | FAQ › Are there things I can't do in a plugin name? |
| FAQ-35 | "Plugins may not start or contain in a way that may be confusing a trademarked term or name of a specific project/library/tool unless submitted by an official representative" | `Plugin Name:` header, readme `=== Name ===` and slug: no third-party trademark/project name at the start or used confusingly (Woo, WordPress, Elementor, Google, ChatGPT …) | FAQ › Are there things I can't do in a plugin name? |
| FAQ-36 | "That error is to inform you that you may not begin your Display Name with someone else's trademarked or common term. This is to protect you and the directory from legal issues regarding trademark abuse and to prevent confusion with 3rd party projects and plugins" | Plugin name (Display Name) must not start with trademark term; grep: readme.txt and main plugin file `Plugin Name:` header | FAQ › Why am I getting an error that says I cannot begin my plugin name with a term? |
| FAQ-37 | "Please do not try to 'work around' this by cleverly renaming your plugin (WuuCommerce for example). All that does is make us worry you're not going to be able to follow guidelines in the future" | No phonetic variants like "Wuu" for "Woo", no leetspeak substitution, no accent removal to bypass trademark blocks; audit `Plugin Name:` string | FAQ › Why am I getting an error that says I cannot begin my plugin name with a term? |
| FAQ-38 | "Some trademark owners have requested we no longer permit the use of specific terms in plugin names entirely. | FAQ-39 | "To proceed with this submission you must remove \"[TERM]\" from the Plugin Name: line in both your main plugin file and readme entirely" | Plugin header `Plugin Name:` in main .php file and readme.txt must not contain prohibited term | FAQ › Why am I getting an error that says I cannot use a term entirely in my plugin name? |
| FAQ-40 | "If you attempt to get around this by changing your term from 'Facerange' to 'Face-Range', we will pend your submission and reiterate that you cannot use the term. | FAQ-41 | "Log in as the official organization user account and submit with that account only" | Process — official plugins submitted from official organization account (verified email domain) | FAQ › How do I submit an official plugin? |
| FAQ-42 | "How we will know that you are the official organization? Because of your email address mostly" | Process — official account email domain must match organization (no @gmail.com, @yahoo.com for org plugins) | FAQ › How do I submit an official plugin? |

| FAQ-44 | "Just reply to the email right away and let us know. We can transfer ownership for you" | Process — not in code; check: contact plugins@wordpress.org immediately if wrong account used | FAQ › What if I submitted the plugin with the wrong user ID? |
| FAQ-45 | "DO NOT resubmit your plugin. Just tell us right away and we'll fix it" | Process — do not resubmit; email plugins@wordpress.org to fix ownership | FAQ › What if I submitted the plugin with the wrong user ID? |
| FAQ-46 | "Put your code files directly in the trunk/ directory of your repository" | SVN structure: code in `/trunk/`, release tags in `/tags/`, assets in `/assets/` at root level | FAQ › Where do I put my files? |
| FAQ-47 | "Make sure you update trunk/readme.txt to reflect the new stable tag" | SVN `/trunk/readme.txt` must have `Stable tag:` pointing to current release in `/tags/` | FAQ › Where do I put my files? |
| FAQ-48 | "Images for the readme (such as screenshots, plugin headers, and plugin icons ), belong in the assets/ directory (which you may need to create) in the root of your SVN checkout. | FAQ-49 | "No. Doing that will cause the zip generator to break" | SVN `/trunk/` structure: main plugin file and `readme.txt` at root, no `/trunk/src/plugin.php` or `/trunk/plugin/plugin.php`; grep: `/trunk/*.php` only | FAQ › Can I put my files in a subdirectory of trunk/? |
| FAQ-50 | "the readme.txt file and the root plugin file should go straight into trunk/" | Plugin entry point .php and `readme.txt` must be at `/trunk/` root, not subdirs | FAQ › Can I put my files in a subdirectory of trunk/? |
| FAQ-51 | "Your Subversion tags should look like version numbers. Specifically, they should only contain numbers and periods" | SVN tag names: `[0-9.]+` only (e.g., `1.0`, `2.8.4`); no letters or descriptive names | FAQ › How should I name my tags (a.k.a. releases)? |
| FAQ-52 | "We recommend you use Semantic Versioning to keep track of releases, but we do not enforce this" | Best practice: SVN `/tags/` names follow MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH (e.g., `1.2.3`); enforced rule: `[0-9.]+` only | FAQ › How should I name my tags (a.k.a. releases)? |
| FAQ-53 | "As few as possible. Very rarely does anyone need your old code in the release repository. Remember, SVN is not meant for your code versioning. You can use Github for stuff like that. SVN should have your current release versions, but you don't need all the minor releases to all the previous versions. Just the last one or two for them is good" | Keep only last 1–2 releases per major version in SVN; archive rest to `changelog.txt` | FAQ › How many old releases should I keep in SVN? |
| FAQ-54 | "No. You can add svn externals to your repository, but they won't get added to the downloadable zip file" | No SVN `svn:externals` property in `/trunk/` or `/tags/`; dependencies must be inlined or composer-bundled | FAQ › Can I include SVN externals in my plugin? |
| FAQ-55 | "No." | No `.zip` or other compressed files in plugin source; grep: no `.zip`, `.tar`, `.gz` files | FAQ › Can I put zips and other compressed files in my plugin? |
