| FAQ-57 | "It's fine to minify, but it's not okay to hide it. All code must be human readable for inclusion in this directory" | Every `.js` and `.css` either non-minified or sourced via readable link in readme | FAQ › Can I include minified JS? |
| FAQ-58 | "As soon as you push code to the SVN folders, your plugin will be live. | FAQ-59 | "As closing a plugin is permanent, we recommend you not push code until you're ready to go live" | Process — plugin closure is permanent; only push tested, release-ready code | FAQ › When does my plugin go 'live'? |
| FAQ-60 | "From the information you specify in the plugin file and in the readme.txt file , and from the Subversion repository itself" | Directory data source: plugin headers (.php) + `readme.txt` + SVN commits | FAQ › Where does the WordPress.org Plugin Directory get its data? |
| FAQ-61 | "You should also make full use of the Plugin Headers in your main plugin file. | FAQ-62 | "Yes, by specifying the Stable Tag field in your trunk directory's readme.txt file" | readme.txt must have `Stable tag:` pointing to a tagged release in SVN `/tags/` | FAQ › Can I specify what version of my plugin the WordPress.org Plugin Directory should use? |
| FAQ-63 | "We ask you not use 'trunk' as your stable tag, as that makes rollbacks more complicated than they need to be" | readme.txt `Stable tag:` must not be 'trunk'; use explicit version (e.g., `1.2.3`) | FAQ › Can I specify what version of my plugin the WordPress.org Plugin Directory should use? |
| FAQ-64 | "Logically, whatever version you tested up to. However, never go above the current release candidate. If there is none, don't go above the active version. If there is a release of 6.1-RC then you may use 6.1, however you can go no higher" | readme.txt `Tested up to:` ≤ current WP version or next RC; no future versions | FAQ › What version of WordPress should the "Tested Up To" value be? |
| FAQ-65 | "Do not attempt to be clever and use 6.5 or 7. This will result in errors on your page" | readme.txt `Tested up to:` must be accurate; false claims cause directory errors | FAQ › What version of WordPress should the "Tested Up To" value be? |
| FAQ-66 | "No. If you're only making cosmetic changes to the readme or your icons/headers, you do not need to release a new version. | FAQ-67 | "Yes. Otherwise no one gets updated" | Each release: main file `Version:` increased, readme `Stable tag:` = that version, SVN `/tags/<version>/` exists | FAQ › Do I need to release a new version of my plugin every time I update the code? |
| FAQ-68 | "A changelog is a log or record of all or all notable changes made to your plugin, including records of changes such as bug fixes, new features, etc." | readme.txt must include `== Changelog ==` section listing all releases and changes | FAQ › What should be in my changelog? |
| FAQ-69 | "Always keep the current major release in your change log. For example, if your current version is 3.9.1, you'll want that and 3.9 in the change log. | FAQ-70 | "That will allow them to be accessible to users, while keeping your readme shorter and more pertinent. | FAQ-71 | "For YouTube and Vimeo videos, simply paste the video link on a line by itself in your description. Note that the video must be set to allow embedding for the embed process to work. | FAQ-72 | "That happens when you neglected to use a proper 'Tested Up To' value in your headers in your readme. | FAQ-73 | "If the latest major WordPress version is 4.9, then you should have the value 4.9 to indicate compatibility. | FAQ-74 | "Keep in mind, if you put in non-released versions of WordPress (like 6.0) you'll see the same message" | readme.txt `Tested up to:` must be released version; no unreleased versions | FAQ › Why does my plugin say it's not been tested with the most recent WordPress versions? |

| FAQ-76 | "You can make your own plugin headers by uploading the correctly named files into the assets folder" | SVN `/assets/` contains plugin banners (1200×422 px or 772×250 px named `banner-*.png/jpg`), headers | FAQ › How do I make one of those cool banners for my plugin page? |

| FAQ-79 | "Much like your plugin name, we recommend your icons and headers be something unique to you. | FAQ-81 | "This helps prevent spam and tag-stuffing. | FAQ-82 | "In addition, tags that are only used by your plugin won't be shown. | FAQ-83 | "Yes and no. | FAQ-84 | "To change the display name, edit your main plugin file and change the value of \"Plugin Name:\" to the new name. | FAQ-88 | "Consider the real life example of Keurig. | FAQ-89 | "In order to protect you, we need you to tread lightly with recognized brand names and trademarks. | FAQ-90 | "Note: We no longer have permission to permit new plugins to use woo as the start of their permalink, and are required to enforce the use of wc instead" | WooCommerce plugin slug: must start with `wc-`, not `woo-`; e.g., `wc-product-manager`, not `woo-product-manager` | FAQ › Why can't I use someone's trademark/brand as my plugin name? |
| FAQ-91 | "While we understand that companies can and do grant usage permissions, we do not accept them for permalinks for a really important reason: we cannot change your permalink once the plugin is approved. | FAQ-92 | "In order to be forward thinking and proactive about a plugin's long-term life in the directory, we do not accept 'permission.' | FAQ-93 | "It's impossible to change a plugin's URL once it's approved and we warn you about that in multiple places through the process" | Process — not in code; check: slug chosen right before submission (the approved slug = `<slug>/<slug>.php` folder can never change) | FAQ › Can I change my plugin's URL/slug? |
| FAQ-94 | "Due to that, we deny most requests for 'new' plugins to replace old ones just to get a better slug" | Process — new plugin submission for slug change rejected; users not migrated between plugins | FAQ › Can I change my plugin's URL/slug? |
| FAQ-95 | "This is because we cannot migrate users between plugins nor can we redirect traffic. | FAQ-96 | "Unless there's an egregious typo, language, or legal issue related to your slug, we are unlikely to approve a new slug. | FAQ-97 | "You'll need to change it in the readme and the plugin main file" | Display name in readme.txt and main plugin file `Plugin Name:` header must match | FAQ › How do I change my plugin's display name? |
| FAQ-98 | "Don't use vulgarities or slurs or other intentionally abusive language. | FAQ-99 | "For example, if you've made a plugin that connects to the Frozbaz Service, you should call your plugin \"Connector to Frozbaz Service\" – in this way, you have made it clear you are making a plugin for a service, rather than being the service" | Plugin name format for integrations: `X Connector for Y`, `X for Y`, not `Y X`; clarifies non-affiliation | FAQ › Can I make my display name anything? |
| FAQ-100 | "If you're combining multiple services (a payment gateway to a popular ecommerce plugin, for example), we strongly recommend you come up with an original, unique, display name" | Multi-service plugins: original, unique `Plugin Name:` over generic "Multi Service Manager"; grep: no generic/template description | FAQ › Can I make my display name anything? |
| FAQ-101 | "Currently yes, but you shouldn't. It's incredibly redundant and doesn't actually help your SEO in any way, shape, or form. | FAQ-102 | "Assuming you actually did apply for trademarks, you certainly can but it's not commonly done. Not even Google or Facebook do that. | FAQ-103 | "Usually 6 to 14 days after a plugin is committed to SVN. | FAQ-104 | "Also as a new plugin, we have no data on usage, so you may need to wait a bit" | Process — new plugins rank low initially; rankings improve with active installations, ratings, support responsiveness | FAQ › How long will it take for my plugin to show up in search? |
| FAQ-105 | "Write a good readme for the language, answer support posts promptly, get good reviews" | Process — search ranking factors: readme.txt quality, forum support response time, user ratings/reviews | FAQ › How do I rank higher? |
| FAQ-106 | "Neither. Make your display name memorable and descriptive, while keeping it under 5 words, for maximum benefit" | `Plugin Name:`/readme `=== Name ===`: under 5 words, descriptive | FAQ › What's weighted more, my URL or my display name? |

| FAQ-108 | "As of April 2020, you can close your own plugins at any time. | FAQ-109 | "Keep in mind, you will not get your plugin restored unless you can justify your situation. | FAQ-110 | "Email plugins@wordpress.org and ask to please have your plugin reopened. | FAQ-111 | "Assuming you're logged in as the correct account, it's probably because you have too many users. | FAQ-112 | "No. We do not permit this as it creates a poor experience for users. | FAQ-113 | "Generally people want to do this when their plugin has a bug that is being fixed, or when they're unable to support it. | FAQ-114 | "If this is for a brand new plugin, you should just call it a 'public beta' so people are aware of the status" | Alternative to closure: readme.txt `Stable tag:` and description can mark plugin as "Public Beta" or "Beta" for new plugins | FAQ › Can I temporarily close my plugin? |
| FAQ-115 | "When a plugin is closed, the page shows as closed and the zips are no longer generated. | FAQ-116 | "After 60 days, the closure message will change to alert people as to why it was closed but only in the broadest terms (Guideline Violation, Security, etc) and not with explicit details" | Process — closure reason disclosed after 60 days in broad terms only (Guideline Violation, Security, etc.); no specific details | FAQ › What happens when a plugin is closed? |
| FAQ-117 | "Plugins are closed for guideline violations, security issues, or by author requests. | FAQ-118 | "If a plugin has never been used within 6 months (i.e. no code has been pushed to SVN), SVN is broken for upwards of 12 months, or a plugin's readme indicates it's deprecated, we may close without notification" | Process — auto-closure: no SVN commit for 6+ months, broken SVN >12 months, or `Deprecated: Yes` in readme; no notice given | FAQ › Why was my plugin closed? |
| FAQ-119 | "If you report an security issue or a guideline violation in a plugin to plugins@wordpress.org, we will review and take appropriate action. | FAQ-120 | "Email plugins@wordpress.org with a link to the stolen plugin. Include either a link to where we can download yours or attach the zip. What do I do? |
| FAQ-121 | "Keep in mind, if you licensed your plugin as GPLv2 or later, then it's perfectly permissible to fork your work, as long as copyright remains intact and you're credited" | `License:` header `GPLv2+` allows forking if copyright header and credits included; no closure | FAQ › Someone posted a copy of my plugin! What do I do? |
| FAQ-122 | "Email plugins@wordpress.org right away! Especially if your code was non-GPL. | FAQ-123 | "We do our best to uphold copyright and trademark requirements, as well as prevent brand confusion. Before plugin are approved, we often require them to make some of the more obvious changes. That said, there is a limit to how 'different' a URL or name can be when we have 60,000 plugins in the directory, and when some terms are quite common (like 'popup' or 'all-in-one')" | Process — trademark enforcement is case-by-case; generic terms like "popup", "all-in-one" not strictly enforceable | FAQ › Will you close another plugin for violating a brand/trademark? |
| FAQ-124 | "If someone is clearly infringing on your copyright or trademark or existing brand, be it by display name or use of trademarked images, please email us at plugins@wordpress.org with some proof and we will contact the developer and require changes" | Process — trademark/copyright infringement reports to plugins@wordpress.org with evidence; developer required to change | FAQ › Will you close another plugin for violating a brand/trademark? |
| FAQ-125 | "We do expect these to be reasonable requests. That is, if you send us a complaint and list 12 plugins that all use the term 'best contact form' because that's your plugin name, we will review the plugins and only close them if they're using the phrase excessively. If they use it once (i.e. \"This is the best contact form plugin in the Faroe Islands\") then it's acceptable. If they're keyword stuffing the phrase, we're more likely to close them for keyword stuffing" | Process — generic term complaints reviewed case-by-case; closure only for keyword stuffing; one-off use in description is acceptable | FAQ › Will you close another plugin for violating a brand/trademark? |
| FAQ-126 | "Simply, if your plugin name is super generic, this is going to happen, and it's usually not an infringement case" | Generic `Plugin Name:` (generic words like "Contacts", "Calendar") invite similar names; not trademark infringement | FAQ › Will you close another plugin for violating a brand/trademark? |
| FAQ-127 | "Also note that if it's not your trademark, we cannot accept your report. It is the responsibility of the trademark owners, not it's users, to manage and maintain that" | Process — only trademark owner/legal representative can report violations; users cannot file reports | FAQ › Will you close another plugin for violating a brand/trademark? |
| FAQ-128 | "Email plugins@wordpress.org a clear and concise description of the issue. Please read our document on reporting security issues for details" | Process — security reports to plugins@wordpress.org; refer to security reporting guidelines | FAQ › How can I send a security report? |
| FAQ-129 | "No. We have no relationship with any bug bounty programs, so we don't file your reports etc to them. The only one with which we work is hackerone.com/automattic and that's for bugs related to Automattic properties. Everything else is on your own, don't ask us to submit things" | Process — no bug bounty program integration; reporter responsible for handling and filing; HackerOne only for Automattic products | FAQ › Do you provide bounties for finding bugs in a plugin? |
| FAQ-130 | "No. We do not have the ability to assist with CVEs" | Process — no CVE assistance; reporter responsible | FAQ › Do you help file or provide CVEs? |
| FAQ-131 | "Maybe. If it was closed for a security reason, fix the issue, reply to the email, and most of the time we'll reopen the plugin unless it has more security issues or severe guideline issues" | Process — closed for security: fix issue + email `plugins@wordpress.org` reply = likely reopening (if no new security issues) | FAQ › My plugin was closed, can I reopen it? |
| FAQ-132 | "If it was closed for guideline violations, it depends on the severity and nature of the violation. Repeat offenders are less likely to have a plugin reopened, for example, than first-timers" | Process — closed for guidelines: reopening depends on violation severity and history; repeat offenders less likely | FAQ › My plugin was closed, can I reopen it? |
| FAQ-133 | "If you asked for the plugin to be closed, you will be expected to explain why the change of heart. Plugins are intended to remain closed when a developer requests it, and not reopened again a month later" | Process — author-requested closure: reinstatement requires justification email; not to be closed/reopened repeatedly | FAQ › My plugin was closed, can I reopen it? |
| FAQ-134 | "All plugins must pass a current standards and security review in order to be restored. This is not optional. Users will lose more faith in you for having your plugin closed multiple times than they would for one longer closure where you address all the potential issues" | Process — closed plugin reinstatement: must pass full current security and guideline audit (mandatory, not optional) | FAQ › My plugin was closed, can I reopen it? |
| FAQ-135 | "Everyone who represents a plugin, from support tech to developer, is the responsibility of the plugin owner. If they violate the guidelines egregiously, then the owners are expected to accept those consequences and correct course. When that doesn't happen, plugins get closed" | Process — plugin owner is liable for all committers/contributors; egregious staff violations = plugin closure | FAQ › Why was my plugin closed when it was my employee/co-worker who violated guidelines? |
| FAQ-136 | "We notify the plugin owners in these cases and explain why and do our best to keep plugins open" | Process — owner notified of staff violations with explanation; closure is last resort | FAQ › Why was my plugin closed when it was my employee/co-worker who violated guidelines? |
| FAQ-137 | "It's exceptionally rare that we close all of a developer's plugins. In general it happens because of the following: ... You asked us to close all your plugins ... Email issues ... The email bounced and we were unable to get in touch ... The email sent us auto-replies and warnings were sent at least twice to fix that" | Process — mass closure rare; triggers: author request, email bounce (after 2+ warnings), auto-reply uncorrected | FAQ › All my plugins were closed! How can I get them back? |
| FAQ-138 | "Guideline issues ... Previous censuring for behaviour and/or a final warning was issued ... Delivering legal threats to the directory and/or the volunteers ... The violation was deemed 'egregious' (death threats, hundreds of sock puppets, harassment, etc)" | Process — mass closure triggers: guideline breaches post-warning, legal threats to volunteers, egregious conduct (death threats, sockpuppeting, harassment) | FAQ › All my plugins were closed! How can I get them back? |
| FAQ-139 | "If you asked us to close them, you have to explain why the change of heart" | Process — author-requested mass closure: reinstatement requires justification email | FAQ › All my plugins were closed! How can I get them back? |
| FAQ-140 | "If you're having email issues, you have to resolve them and you'll be required to bring all your plugins up to current standards of security and guidelines" | Process — mass closure for email issues: fix contact info on all plugins + pass full security and guideline audit | FAQ › All my plugins were closed! How can I get them back? |
| FAQ-141 | "As for that last one … Generally you don't get to come back from that. If we deliver you a final warning for your behaviour and, within less than a year, you start up again with the issues (or fail to resolve all the issues we mentioned), we're not going to reopen your plugins" | Process — final warning: repeated violations within 1 year post-warning = no reinstatement | FAQ › All my plugins were closed! How can I get them back? |
| FAQ-142 | "First and foremost, take it seriously. The email will list exactly what the problems have been and why we've chosen to escalate to a final warning. Plugin Owners are expected to resolve all the issues, to cease causing new guideline violations, and to closely monitor the actions of any coworkers. In short, stop breaking the guidelines, stop making excuses, apologize for any misbehaviour, and correct course" | Process — final warning response: resolve all listed issues, prevent new violations, monitor staff conduct, no excuses | FAQ › I just got a final warning. What do I do? |
| FAQ-143 | "The last thing we want to do is ban someone and disable all their plugins. It's not healthy for the community. At the same time, if a developer is unable or unwilling to play by the same rules as everyone else, it's detrimental to keep then in the directory and disrespectful to everyone else" | Process — final warning escalation: account ban + all plugins closure if compliance fails | FAQ › I just got a final warning. What do I do? |
| FAQ-144 | "To add users as committers, that is give them access to update code, go to https://wordpress.org/plugins/YOURPLUGIN/advanced and add their username in as a committer" | Process — add committer via Advanced tab; committers have SVN commit access | FAQ › How do I give someone else access to my plugin? |
| FAQ-145 | "To have them show up as an author, add their username to the readme.txt file" | readme.txt `Contributors:` or `Author:` line lists all contributors | FAQ › How do I give someone else access to my plugin? |
| FAQ-146 | "Do not add regular users as authors. It's meant for people who help with development only. This means if someone 'inspired' you, you should not add them as an author" | readme.txt `Author:` line or SVN committer: reserved for developers only; not supporters/testers/funders | FAQ › How do I give someone else access to my plugin? |
| FAQ-147 | "The leadership of the WordPress project and the Plugin Review team will review each case individually" | Process — blocked owner: case-by-case review for plugin disposition | FAQ › What happens to a plugin if the plugin owner gets blocked? |
| FAQ-148 | "Not having a new owner for a plugin can have a lot of security implications, as users would no longer be able to receive new updates" | Process — ownerless plugin (owner blocked): security risk, no updates possible; team decides closure, transfer, or adoption | FAQ › What happens to a plugin if the plugin owner gets blocked? |
| FAQ-149 | "When the plugin doesn't have a lot of active installations or is only necessary for specific use cases, the team will likely just close it" | Process — low-adoption orphaned plugin (owner blocked): likely closed | FAQ › What happens to a plugin if the plugin owner gets blocked? |
| FAQ-150 | "Whenever we have a plugin that is relevant enough to become a community/canonical project, it could be transferred to the wordpressdotorg user" | Process — high-value orphaned plugin (owner blocked): may transfer to `wordpressdotorg` canonical account | FAQ › What happens to a plugin if the plugin owner gets blocked? |
| FAQ-151 | "In an application process managed by the team, it could be possibly donated to a different user if approved by the WordPress project leadership" | Process — high-value orphaned plugin (owner blocked): may donate to qualified adopter with leadership approval | FAQ › What happens to a plugin if the plugin owner gets blocked? |
| FAQ-152 | "Anyone with commit access can do this. Go to https://wordpress.org/plugins/YOURPLUGIN/advanced and hover over their ID. A delete link will appear. Click on it" | Process — remove committer via Advanced tab (any committer can remove others) | FAQ › How do I remove someone's access from my plugin? |
| FAQ-153 | "Please don't delete yourself" | Process — do not remove own committer access | FAQ › How do I remove someone's access from my plugin? |
| FAQ-154 | "Go to the Advanced tab and scroll down to the Danger Zone. There you will see a section for Transfer Your Plugin. Pick someone from the dropdown and click the button" | Process — transfer ownership via Advanced tab Danger Zone | FAQ › How do I change the plugin owner? |
| FAQ-155 | "Plugins with a large number of users (over 10,000) or ones that are deemed critical to the WordPress project (such as featured or beta plugins) can only be transfered via written request to the plugins team. Please read the documentation on transfering plugins for details" | Process — plugin with >10,000 users or critical plugin (featured/beta): transfer via email to `plugins@wordpress.org` only (no UI transfer) | FAQ › I tried to transfer my plugin but it says I can't. Why not? |
| FAQ-156 | "We permit users to adopt existing plugins that are no longer currently developed" | Process — abandoned plugin adoption permitted; contact original developer first | FAQ › How can I take over an abandoned plugin? |
| FAQ-157 | "We ask you try to connect with the original developers first, so they can add you. In some case, that's not possible and you should start with fixing the plugin. Make sure it meets coding standards, is secure, and update the copyright information to include yourself" | Process — adoption: contact original dev first; fix security/standards; update readme.txt copyright header to include self | FAQ › How can I take over an abandoned plugin? |
| FAQ-158 | "Then you can contact us regarding plugin adoption" | Process — after fixes: email plugins@wordpress.org for adoption approval | FAQ › How can I take over an abandoned plugin? |
| FAQ-159 | "We offer no guarantee that you will be given anyone's plugin, even following a successful review" | Process — adoption not guaranteed even after successful review; team discretion | FAQ › How can I take over an abandoned plugin? |
| FAQ-160 | "Short answer: Probably not. Many developers receive unsolicited emails or offers to purchase their plugin. We have found the vast majority of these to be fraudulent and do not recommend you follow up with them" | Process — plugin purchase offers: majority fraudulent; do not respond to unsolicited emails | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-161 | "While legitimate offers do come, they're usually from the official company to whom a plugin is related, or from a well established plugin company. The ones that start \"We're reaching out to the WordPress community …\" or \"We are looking to acquire existing WordPress plugins …\" should not be trusted" | Process — legitimate offers from official companies or established firms only; generic "reaching out" emails = fraud | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-162 | "Such purchases have often destroyed the reputation of the plugin (and the original developer) by engaging in sleazy tactics such as tracking users or other serious guideline violations" | Process — sale risk: buyer may add tracking, spam, violate guidelines, damaging original dev reputation | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-163 | "If you do choose to sell your plugin (or give it away to someone else), please make sure the new owners understand all the guidelines of the repository" | Process — seller responsible: vet buyer compliance with all directory guidelines before transfer | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-164 | "Should they violate our terms the plugin will be removed, and we may not give it back depending on the level of the violation. Whomever has commit access to a plugin has the ownership and responsibility of it's behavior for users" | Process — new owner violations = closure; all committers liable for plugin conduct | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-165 | "Spamming, inserting tracking data, and adding junk features are the fastest way to ruin your plugin" | Process — worst buyer behavior: spam, tracking, low-value features = immediate closure | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-166 | "We advocate only giving your plugin to people you personally have vetted, and that you trust with being responsible with your code and your users" | Process — only transfer to personally vetted, trusted individuals | FAQ › Are these offers to buy my plugin legit? |
| FAQ-167 | "When a developer is determined to have died, they are removed from their own plugins in order to prevent the unethical from gaining access and harming users. If they are the only developer, the plugin may be closed" | Process — developer death: committers removed; closure if sole developer and no adoption | FAQ › What happens when a plugin developer dies? |
| FAQ-168 | "All attempts are made to find their friends and coworkers, to offer them a chance to adopt the code first, but if no one reliable or willing can be found the plugin is closed" | Process — developer death: team contacts friends/coworkers for adoption; closure if no qualified adopter | FAQ › What happens when a plugin developer dies? |

## header-requirements.txt — Header Requirements
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/plugin-basics/header-requirements/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-1 | "the main PHP file should include header comment what tells WordPress that a file is a plugin and provides information about the plugin." | Main plugin file at the plugin root opens with a `/* … */` or `/** … */` block holding `Plugin Name:`; `grep -l "Plugin Name:" *.php` returns exactly one root file | Header Requirements › intro |
| DEV-2 | "At a minimum, a header comment must contain the Plugin Name:" | `Plugin Name:` present and non-empty in the main file header | Header Requirements › Minimum Fields |
| DEV-3 | "Plugin Name: (required) The name of your plugin, which will be displayed in the Plugins list in the WordPress Admin." | `Plugin Name:` value matches the readme `=== Name ===` title and the intended display name | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-4 | "Plugin URI: The home page of the plugin, which should be a unique URL, preferably on your own website." | `Plugin URI:` points to a page specific to this plugin (not a generic homepage) | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-5 | "This must be unique to your plugin. You cannot use a WordPress.org URL here." | `Plugin URI:` must not contain `wordpress.org`; must differ from `Author URI:` | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-6 | "Description: A short description of the plugin, as displayed in the Plugins section in the WordPress Admin. Keep this description to fewer than 140 characters." | Length of `Description:` value < 140 characters | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-7 | "Version: The current version number of the plugin, such as 1.0 or 1.0.3." | `Version:` present, numeric dotted form; equals readme `Stable tag:` and the SVN `/tags/<version>/` folder; equals any version constant in code | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-8 | "Requires at least: The lowest WordPress version that the plugin will work on." | `Requires at least:` present; no calls to WP functions introduced after that version (e.g. check `wp_` APIs against their `@since`) | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-9 | "Requires PHP: The minimum required PHP version." | `Requires PHP:` present; no syntax newer than that version (typed properties, `match`, `?->`, enums, `readonly`) | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-10 | "Author: The name of the plugin author. Multiple authors may be listed using commas." | `Author:` present; multiple names comma-separated | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-11 | "Author URI: The author's website or profile on another website, such as WordPress.org." | `Author URI:` is a valid URL to the author's site or profile | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-12 | "License: The short name (slug) of the plugin's license (e.g. GPLv2)." | `License:` present with a GPL-compatible short name (e.g. `GPLv2 or later`, `GPL-2.0-or-later`); matches readme `License:` | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-13 | "License URI: A link to the full text of the license (e.g. https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-2.0.html )." | `License URI:` present and points at the full licence text matching `License:` | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-14 | "Text Domain: The gettext  text domain of the plugin." | `Text Domain:` = plugin slug (folder name); every `__(`, `_e(`, `esc_html__(`, `_x(`, `_n(` and JS `__(` uses that same domain string | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-15 | "Domain Path: The domain path lets WordPress know where to find the translations." | If `Domain Path:` is set (e.g. `/languages`), that folder exists in the package | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-16 | "Network: Whether the plugin can only be activated network-wide." | `Network:` present only if the plugin truly must be network-activated | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-17 | "Can only be set to true, and should be left out when not needed." | `Network:` value is exactly `true` or the line is absent; never `false` | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-18 | "Update URI: Allows third-party plugins to avoid accidentally being overwritten with an update of a plugin of a similar name from the WordPress.org Plugin Directory." | `Update URI:` — a plugin hosted on wordpress.org should not set it to a non-wordpress.org value (not in this source; cross-check the Detailed Guidelines rows) | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-19 | "Requires Plugins: A comma-separated list of WordPress.org-formatted slugs for its dependencies, such as `my-plugin`" | `Requires Plugins:` value is a comma-separated list of bare slugs that exist on wordpress.org | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-20 | "(`my-plugin/my-plugin.php` is not supported)" | `Requires Plugins:` contains no `/` and no `.php` | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-21 | "It does not support commas in plugin slugs." | No dependency slug in `Requires Plugins:` contains a comma | Header Requirements › Header Fields |
| DEV-22 | "Here's another example which allows file-level PHPDoc DocBlock as well as WordPress plugin file headers" | If a PHPDoc DocBlock precedes the header, the header fields follow an `@wordpress-plugin` line in the same comment | Header Requirements › Header Fields (example 2) |
| DEV-23 | "WordPress uses the PHP version_compare() function to compare plugin version numbers. Therefore, before you release a new version of your plugin, you should make sure that this PHP function considers the new version to be “greater” than the old one.  For example, 1.02 is actually greater than 1.1." | `php -r "var_dump(version_compare('NEW','OLD','>'));"` is `true` for new `Version:` vs last tag | Header Requirements › Notes |

## compliance-disclaimers.txt — Compliance Disclaimers
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/compliance-disclaimers/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-24 | "Guideline 9  (Developers and their plugins must not do anything illegal, dishonest, or morally offensive.) has been amended to include the following new prohibition:" | Readme, header `Description:`, admin strings: no dishonest claims (see next rows) | Compliance Disclaimers › intro |
| DEV-25 | "implying that a plugin can create, provide, automate, or guarantee legal compliance" | Grep readme, `Plugin Name:`, `Description:`, assets text and UI strings for "compliant", "compliance", "GDPR", "ADA", "WCAG", "PCI", "HIPAA", "VAT", "guarantee" | Compliance Disclaimers › intro |
| DEV-26 | "No plugin can offer 100% legal compliance." | Grep for "100%" near "compliant"/"compliance" in readme and strings | Compliance Disclaimers › intro |
| DEV-27 | "at the end of the day, the responsibility remains with the site administrators to ensure their sites meet the qualifications for any compliance." | Readme does not shift legal responsibility to the plugin; wording leaves it with the site owner | Compliance Disclaimers › intro |
| DEV-28 | "we recommend that plugins do not claim to be 100% compliant, and instead to explain that the plugin itself will assist in compliance." | Compliance wording reads "helps"/"assists"/"more compliant", not "is compliant"/"makes compliant" | Compliance Disclaimers › intro |
| DEV-29 | "Update your readme, documentation, assets (banners, screenshots, etc), and descriptions to clearly state that your plugin is meant to assist in compliance." | Check readme, docs, `assets/banner-*`, `assets/screenshot-*` text and `Description:` for assist-wording | Compliance Disclaimers › What do I need to do? |
| DEV-30 | "if your plugin says it "will make your website ADA compliant." you should change that to "will help make your website more ADA compliant."" | Grep "will make your (website\|site) .* compliant", "makes your site compliant" | Compliance Disclaimers › What do I need to do? |
| DEV-31 | "it would be wise to add in a note that "no plugin can provide 100% compliance" and then enumerate what yours does to get people closer." | Readme carries a "no plugin can provide 100% compliance" note plus a list of what the plugin does | Compliance Disclaimers › What do I need to do? |
| DEV-32 | "If your plugin name claims or implies compliance, yes." | `Plugin Name:` and readme title must not claim or imply compliance (e.g. "… Compliance" → "… Compliance Assistant") | Compliance Disclaimers › Do I need to change my plugin display name? |
| DEV-33 | "a BETTER plugin name would be one that is unique to you." | `Plugin Name:` is distinctive (brand-prefixed), not a generic descriptor | Compliance Disclaimers › Do I need to change my plugin display name? |
| DEV-34 | "A service assumes the responsibility for the compliance needed in those cases, and that's what needs to be clear in the readme: it's not the plugin code, it's the service." | For SaaS-backed plugins the readme attributes any compliance to the named service, not to the plugin code | Compliance Disclaimers › My plugin's a service… |
| DEV-35 | "We strongly recommend you link to your proof of compliance, and that is has a date on it." | Readme links to a dated compliance proof page of the service | Compliance Disclaimers › My plugin's a service… |
| DEV-36 | "Not unless you also claim it in your own readme/documentation." | Third-party library compliance claims must not be repeated in own readme/docs | Compliance Disclaimers › My plugin uses a 3rd party library… |
| DEV-37 | "Technically no, but … can you really promise 100% compliance forever and ever? Probably best to change that and just say "Updated for further XHTML compliance."" | Grep "100% (XHTML\|W3C\|valid)" in readme; prefer "further … compliance" | Compliance Disclaimers › I only talk about 100% XHTML compliance… |
| DEV-38 | "If we find you're making inaccurate claims, we will warn you. Then if it's not fixed in a reasonable amount of time (60 days) your plugin may be closed." | Process — check: any warning email about claims is answered and fixed within 60 days | Compliance Disclaimers › What happens if I don't do this? |
| DEV-39 | "if your plugin is closed for other issues (guideline or security related), we will require you to update the readme before we will reopen the plugin." | Process — check: readme compliance wording fixed before any reopen request | Compliance Disclaimers › What happens if I don't do this? |
| DEV-40 | "Email `plugins@wordpress.org` with a link to their plugin and an explanation as to why you feel they're in violation." | Process — check: reports of other plugins go to plugins@wordpress.org with link and reason | Compliance Disclaimers › How do I report other plugins… |
| DEV-41 | "pointing out other people making this mistake doesn't give you a free pass. You have to fix your own stuff too." | Own readme/assets audited regardless of competitors' wording | Compliance Disclaimers › How do I report other plugins… |

## automated-security-review.txt — Automated Security Review
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/automated-security-review/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-42 | "Every new release of a plugin hosted on WordPress.org goes through an automated security review before it is distributed through the WordPress.org update API." | Before tagging, diff the release against the last tag and self-review: `eval(`, `base64_decode(`, `$_GET`/`$_POST`/`$_REQUEST` without `sanitize_`/`absint`/`wp_unslash`, `echo` without `esc_`, `$wpdb->query(`/`get_results(` without `prepare(`, REST `permission_callback` → `__return_true` on writes | Automated Security Review › intro |
| DEV-43 | "Releases with a high risk score are blocked from distribution until the issues are resolved." | Same diff review; also `wp_ajax_nopriv_` handlers, missing `check_ajax_referer`/`wp_verify_nonce`, missing `current_user_can(` | Automated Security Review › intro |
| DEV-44 | "Since June 2026, every plugin release goes through a cooldown period  before it is distributed through the WordPress.org update API" | Process — check: plan release timing to allow the cooldown before users see the update | Automated Security Review › How Releases Are Reviewed |
| DEV-45 | "During the cooldown, the changes in each release are analyzed by several AI models together with Jetpack Scan." | Review only the changed files of the release (`svn diff tags/<old> trunk`) for malware-like and vulnerable patterns: `eval(`, `assert(`, `create_function(`, `preg_replace` with `/e`, obfuscated strings, `file_put_contents(` from request data, `unserialize(` of input | Automated Security Review › How Releases Are Reviewed |
| DEV-46 | "Below the blocking threshold: The release continues through the normal cooldown process and is distributed as usual. No email is sent, and no action is needed from plugin authors." | Process — check: no email = no action | Automated Security Review › How Releases Are Reviewed |
| DEV-47 | "At or above the blocking threshold: The release is blocked from distribution as soon as the review finishes, and all committers of the plugin receive an email with the findings." | Process — check: all committers' inboxes monitored after each release | Automated Security Review › How Releases Are Reviewed |
| DEV-48 | "A high risk score does not mean malicious intent. An accidentally introduced vulnerability can score just as high as intentional malware. The score measures risk, not intent." | Treat accidental vulnerabilities as release blockers: unescaped output, unsanitized input, missing nonce/capability checks, SQL without `prepare(` | Automated Security Review › How Releases Are Reviewed |
| DEV-49 | "It includes the highest risk score the review reported and a list of findings, each with its own risk score, a short summary, and the affected file and line" | Process — check: fix each listed file:line from the email | Automated Security Review › Email Notifications |
| DEV-50 | "Emails are currently only sent when a release is blocked. Authors who haven’t received an email don’t need to take any action." | Process — check: none | Automated Security Review › Email Notifications |
| DEV-51 | "Sites running the plugin are not offered the blocked version as an update. They keep receiving the previously distributed version." | Process — check: a blocked version never reaches users; verify via the update API which version is served | Automated Security Review › What "Blocked" Means |
| DEV-52 | "Only that specific version is blocked. The plugin remains published in the directory, it is not closed, its page stays up, and previously released versions are unaffected." | Process — check: blocked ≠ closed | Automated Security Review › What "Blocked" Means |
| DEV-53 | "The block stays in place beyond the end of the cooldown period until it is resolved in a subsequent release." | Process — check: a block requires a new tagged release; waiting does not clear it | Automated Security Review › What "Blocked" Means |
| DEV-54 | "A blocked release will not be distributed through the update API until the issues are resolved." | Process — check: fix and re-release | Automated Security Review › Resolving a Blocked Release |
| DEV-55 | "Review the reported findings in the email. They describe exactly what triggered the block." | Process — check: read every finding and its file:line | Automated Security Review › Resolving a Blocked Release |
| DEV-56 | "Fix the issues and release a new version as usual. The block only applies to the flagged version, so the new release is unaffected by it and goes through the regular cooldown and security review." | Bump `Version:` and readme `Stable tag:`, create new `/tags/<version>/`; do not re-use the blocked version number | Automated Security Review › Resolving a Blocked Release |
| DEV-57 | "When it scores below the blocking threshold, it is distributed normally." | Process — check: confirm the fixed version is served by the update API | Automated Security Review › Resolving a Blocked Release |
| DEV-58 | "If a finding looks incorrect, plugin authors should reply directly to the notification email explaining why the finding doesn't apply." | Process — check: false positives are answered by replying to the notification email | Automated Security Review › Incorrect Findings |
| DEV-59 | "publishing a fixed release is almost always faster than waiting for the manual review of an appeal." | Process — check: prefer a fixed release over an appeal | Automated Security Review › Incorrect Findings |
| DEV-60 | "Only if the findings turn out to be incorrect after an appeal to the Plugins Team." | Process — check: unblocking without a new version requires a successful appeal | Automated Security Review › FAQ › Can a blocked version be unblocked… |
| DEV-61 | "Every release goes through the same automated review during its cooldown, including releases that fix a blocked one." | Re-run the full self-review on the fix release, not just the flagged lines | Automated Security Review › FAQ › Will the new version be reviewed again? |
| DEV-62 | "A release block is not a closure. … Closures are a separate process and are communicated separately." | Process — check: distinguish block emails from closure emails | Automated Security Review › FAQ › Was my plugin closed? |

## plugin-assets.txt — How Your Plugin Assets Work
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/plugin-assets/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-63 | "All files should be placed into the `assets` directory of your SVN directory and will work for all versions of your plugin." | Banners, icons, screenshots, blueprints live in SVN `/assets/`, not in the plugin zip | Plugin Assets › intro |
| DEV-64 | "This is a top level directory, just like `trunk`. You would not place the screenshots into `trunk/assets` or `tags/1.0/assets`." | No `trunk/assets/` or `tags/*/assets/` holding `banner-*`, `icon-*`, `screenshot-*` | Plugin Assets › intro |
| DEV-65 | "It should only take a few minutes, but 6 hours is not unheard of when the servers are under high load" | Process — check: wait up to 6 hours for CDN before reporting missing images | Plugin Assets › intro |
| DEV-66 | "Image sizes should be the same as implied by the names." | Read pixel dimensions of every `assets/*-WxH.*` file and compare with the name | Plugin Assets › Default Image Sizes |
| DEV-67 | "`banner-772x250.png` should be 772 pixels wide by 250 pixels high." | `assets/banner-772x250.(png\|jpg)` is exactly 772×250 | Plugin Assets › Default Image Sizes |
| DEV-68 | "`icon-256x256.png` should be a 256&#215;256 square." | `assets/icon-256x256.(png\|jpg\|gif)` is exactly 256×256 | Plugin Assets › Default Image Sizes |
| DEV-69 | "We have not defined any new banner sizes so please don't try to be clever and rename yours thinking they'll work. They simply won't show." | Only `banner-772x250*` and `banner-1544x500*` names in `assets/` | Plugin Assets › Default Image Sizes |
| DEV-70 | "don't make your images larger (or smaller) and use the existing names. Things will look terrible." | Dimensions match names for all banners/icons (no oversized file under a standard name) | Plugin Assets › Default Image Sizes |
| DEV-71 | "Ideally, design your banner such that the elements included in the image can be positioned from right to left or from left to right." | Visual check of banner for RTL suitability | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers |
| DEV-72 | "You can create a different image for RTL pages, with `-rtl` in the name." | Optional `assets/banner-772x250-rtl.(png\|jpg)` | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers |
| DEV-73 | "Normal Banner: `banner-772x250.(jpg\|png)`" | `assets/banner-772x250.png` or `.jpg` exists | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-74 | "Normal Banner (Localized): `banner-772x250-(rtl\|es\|es_ES).(jpg\|png)`" | Localized banners follow `banner-772x250-<rtl\|locale>.(jpg\|png)` | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-75 | "High-DPI (Retina): `banner-1544x500.(jpg\|png)`" | `assets/banner-1544x500.(png\|jpg)` is exactly 1544×500 | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-76 | "High-DPI (Retina Localized): `banner-1544x500-(rtl\|es\|es_ES).(jpg\|png)`" | Localized retina banners follow `banner-1544x500-<rtl\|locale>.(jpg\|png)` | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-77 | "The locale can be specified as a full locale (`es_ES`) or as a partial locale (`es`), if the language is RTL and a locale-specific image isn't provided, the `rtl` image will be checked for." | Locale suffix is `xx` or `xx_YY` | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-78 | "Do not duplicate English images into RTL and locale-specific files without making alterations, the English variant will be used instead." | Hash-compare `banner-*-rtl`/`-<locale>` with the English file; identical copies should be removed | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-79 | "You cannot use the retina image alone, it only works as an "add-on" to the 772&#215;250 image." | If `banner-1544x500.*` exists, `banner-772x250.*` must exist too | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-80 | "4MB is the maximum size for headers images, but smaller is better." | Each `assets/banner-*` ≤ 4 MB | Plugin Assets › Plugin Headers › Filenames |
| DEV-81 | "In addition to JPG and PNG formats, you can also use SVG." | Icons are `.png`, `.jpg` (or `.gif` per filename list) or `icon.svg` | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons |
| DEV-82 | "If you chose to use SVGs, you must also use a PNG icon as a fallback" | If `assets/icon.svg` exists, `assets/icon-128x128.png` and/or `icon-256x256.png` must exist | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons |
| DEV-83 | "If you do not use an icon, an auto-generated one will be made for you." | Absence of `assets/icon-*` is allowed (auto icon) | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons |
| DEV-84 | "1MB is the maximum file size for icons, but as with headers, the smaller the better." | Each `assets/icon-*` and `icon.svg` ≤ 1 MB | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons |
| DEV-85 | "Normal: `icon-128x128.(png\|jpg\|gif)`" | `assets/icon-128x128.*` is exactly 128×128 | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons › Filenames |
| DEV-86 | "High-DPI (Retina): `icon-256x256.(png\|jpg\|gif)`" | `assets/icon-256x256.*` is exactly 256×256 | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons › Filenames |
| DEV-87 | "SVG: `icon.svg`" | SVG icon named exactly `assets/icon.svg` | Plugin Assets › Plugin Icons › Filenames |
| DEV-88 | "There should be one screenshot for every line in your `readme.txt` file." | Count lines under readme `== Screenshots ==` = count of `assets/screenshot-N.*` | Plugin Assets › Screenshots |
| DEV-89 | "The content of the lines will become the captions of the screenshots on your plugin's page. For example: `1. This is a monkey`" | Readme `== Screenshots ==` uses `N. Caption` lines, N matching `screenshot-N` | Plugin Assets › Screenshots |
| DEV-90 | "Screenshots must be local to display. Links to external files won't work." | No `http` URLs under readme `== Screenshots ==`; files exist in `assets/` | Plugin Assets › Screenshots |
| DEV-91 | "10MB is maximum file size for screenshots, but as always, the smaller the better." | Each `assets/screenshot-*` ≤ 10 MB | Plugin Assets › Screenshots |
| DEV-92 | "`screenshot-1.(png\|jpg)`" | Screenshots named `screenshot-<N>.png` or `.jpg` | Plugin Assets › Screenshots › Filenames |
| DEV-93 | "All filenames should be lowercase; uppercase names won't work." | `ls assets/` shows no uppercase letters or `.PNG`/`.JPG` extensions | Plugin Assets › Screenshots › Filenames |
| DEV-94 | "the following filenames would take priority over the above English names when the plugin is viewed in German: … `screenshot-1-de.(png\|jpg)`" | Localized screenshots follow `screenshot-<N>-<locale>.(png\|jpg)` | Plugin Assets › Screenshots › Filenames |
| DEV-95 | "If you find your images are downloading when people click on them … It's due to how some images are sent with the `Content-Type application/octet-stream`." | `svn propget svn:mime-type assets/*` is `image/png`/`image/jpeg`, not `application/octet-stream` | Plugin Assets › Issues |
| DEV-96 | "To fix it, run this command: … svn propset svn:mime-type image/png *.png" | Process — check: run `svn propset svn:mime-type image/png *.png` and `image/jpeg *.jpg` on committed assets | Plugin Assets › Issues |
| DEV-97 | "Alternatively, plugin authors can set this in their ~/.subversion/config file … That'll apply to only new files though. Fixing already-committed files will require the command above." | Process — check: `~/.subversion/config` auto-props for new files; propset for existing ones | Plugin Assets › Issues |

## release-confirmation-emails.txt — Release Confirmation Emails
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/release-confirmation-emails/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-98 | "When enabled on a plugin, to release a new version of a plugin you'll need to tag a new release in SVN as normal, and then confirm on the Release Management  dashboard." | Process — check: if enabled, each new `/tags/<version>/` is confirmed on the Release Management dashboard | Release Confirmation Emails › intro |
| DEV-99 | "Access to the Release Management dashboard is limited to plugin committers, and all actions require confirmation via a tokenised link which is emailed to you as needed." | Process — check: the confirming person is a committer with working email | Release Confirmation Emails › intro |
| DEV-100 | "When WordPress.org detects a new version has been tagged in SVN, all committers are automatically sent an email notifying them that a new release is pending, who made it, and a link to the dashboard to confirm the release." | Process — check: an unexpected "release pending" email means an unauthorised tag | Release Confirmation Emails › intro |
| DEV-101 | "Once confirmed, the release will proceed as usual and should update within a few minutes." | Process — check: verify the served version after confirming | Release Confirmation Emails › intro |
| DEV-102 | "Plugins are required to use tags for new versions, you cannot release your plugin from trunk." | Readme `Stable tag:` is a version number, not `trunk`; matching `/tags/<version>/` exists | Release Confirmation Emails › Requirements |
| DEV-103 | "You must still update the `Stable Tag:` header in your trunk/readme.txt file." | `trunk/readme.txt` `Stable tag:` = new `Version:` | Release Confirmation Emails › Requirements |
| DEV-104 | "Once released, alterations cannot be made to the tagged version. This includes changing the readme (for tested up to)." | Process — check: `Tested up to:` and readme fixes are done before tagging; a tagged release is frozen | Release Confirmation Emails › Requirements |
| DEV-105 | "Committers must be able to receive emails directly, and not have it go to a shared inbox or support ticket system." | Process — check: each committer account's email is a personal direct inbox | Release Confirmation Emails › Requirements |
| DEV-106 | "Release Confirmations are opt-in, but only requires a singular committer to confirm the release." | Process — check: whether the opt-in is enabled on the plugin's Advanced view | Release Confirmation Emails › Requiring double approval |
| DEV-107 | "If a group would like to require multiple committers to approve a release, please contact the Plugins team with your request." | Process — check: request double approval from the Plugins team if wanted | Release Confirmation Emails › Requiring double approval |

## how-to-use-subversion.txt — Using Subversion
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/how-to-use-subversion/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-108 | "SVN and the Plugin Directory are a release repository. Unlike Git, you shouldn't commit every small change, as doing so can degrade performance. Please only push finished changes to your SVN repository." | Process — check: `svn log` shows release-sized commits, not a stream of small ones | Using Subversion › intro |
| DEV-109 | "Tags are great for labeling different releases of your plugin  and are the only fully supported method of ensuring the correct versions are seen on WordPress.org and updated for users." | Every released `Version:` has a `/tags/<version>/` folder | Using Subversion › Overview |
| DEV-110 | "Your account for SVN will be the same username (not the email) of the account you used when you submitted the plugin." | Process — check: SVN login uses the wordpress.org username, not the email | Using Subversion › Your Account |
| DEV-111 | "WordPress.org allows setting a SVN-specific password for your account, this can be done in your Account Settings ." | Process — check: SVN password set in Account Settings | Using Subversion › Your Account |
| DEV-112 | "Remember, capitalization matters — if your username is JaneDoe, then you must use the capital J and D or else SVN will fail." | Process — check: `--username` matches the profile's exact capitalisation | Using Subversion › Your Account |
| DEV-113 | "Use `assets` for screenshots, plugin headers, and plugin icons ." | `banner-*`, `icon-*`, `screenshot-*` only under SVN `/assets/` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders |
| DEV-114 | "Development work belongs in `trunk`." | `/trunk/` holds the current code | Using Subversion › SVN Folders |
| DEV-115 | "Releases go in `tags`." | Each release in `/tags/<version>/` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders |
| DEV-116 | "Do not put your main plugin file in a subfolder of trunk, like `/trunk/my-plugin/my-plugin.php` as that will break downloads. You may use subfolders for included files." | The file with `Plugin Name:` sits directly in `/trunk/` (and at the zip root folder), not in `/trunk/<slug>/` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Trunk |
| DEV-117 | "The `/trunk` directory is where your plugin code should live." | `/trunk/` is not empty or a placeholder | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Trunk |
| DEV-118 | "Even if you do your development work elsewhere (like a git repository), we recommend you keep the trunk folder up to date with your code for easy SVN compares." | `svn diff` trunk vs latest tag is empty or only unreleased work | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Trunk |
| DEV-119 | "You will use the same version numbers for the sub-directories here as you do for your plugin versioning." | `/tags/<version>/` name = `Version:` header in that tag's main file = readme `Stable tag:` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Tags |
| DEV-120 | "It is important that you always use tag folders and proper versioning to ensure your users get the correct code." | No release served from trunk; `Stable tag:` ≠ `trunk` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Tags |
| DEV-121 | "Version 1.0 of the plugin will be in `/tags/1.0`, version 1.1 would be in `/tags/1.1`, and so forth." | Tag folder names are bare version numbers (no `v` prefix) | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Tags |
| DEV-122 | "We strongly encourage the use of semantic software versioning ." | `Version:` follows `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Tags |
| DEV-123 | "Some older plugins in the directory may have screenshot files in /trunk instead, however this is not recommended. All new plugins should put their screenshots in /assets." | No `screenshot-*.png`/`.jpg` inside `/trunk/` or the plugin zip | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Assets |
| DEV-124 | "The WordPress.org system does not use the branches directory for anything at all" | Process — check: nothing released from `/branches/` | Using Subversion › SVN Folders › Branches |
| DEV-125 | "SVN is meant to be a release system, not a development system. You don't need to commit and push every small change, and in fact doing so is detrimental to the system." | Process — check: commit once per release | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Don't use SVN for development |
| DEV-126 | "Every time you push code to SVN, it rebuilds all your zip files for all versions in SVN. … Instead, you should push one time, when you're ready to go." | Process — check: single commit for trunk + new tag | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Don't use SVN for development |
| DEV-127 | "While it's possible to simply update the `readme.txt` file in trunk and put everything in tag folders, doing so makes it more difficult to compare any changes in your code. Instead, trunk should contain the latest version of your code, even if that version is a beta." | `/trunk/` contains full plugin code, at least as new as the latest tag | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Use the trunk folder for code |
| DEV-128 | "While it's possible to use trunk as a stable tag for plugins, this feature is not actually supported nor recommended." | Readme `Stable tag:` is not `trunk` | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Always Tag Releases |
| DEV-129 | "Instead, releases should be properly tagged and iterated." | Each release increments `Version:` and adds a new tag | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Always Tag Releases |
| DEV-130 | "Instead of pushing your code directly to a tag folder, you should edit  the code in trunk, complete with the stable version in the readme, and then copy the code from trunk to the new tag." | Process — check: tag created by copying trunk after `Stable tag:` updated; `svn diff trunk tags/<version>` empty right after release | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Create tags from trunk |
| DEV-131 | "You can use `svn cp` to copy trunk to the tag and then push them up to SVN at the same time." | Process — check: `svn cp trunk tags/<version>` then one `svn ci` | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Create tags from trunk |
| DEV-132 | "as long as you use svn to do the copy operation, then it preserves history and makes everything easy for others to follow along with." | Process — check: `svn log -v tags/<version>` shows "copied from trunk" | Using Subversion › Best Practices › Create tags from trunk |
| DEV-133 | "It's required to include a commit message for all checkins." | Process — check: every `svn ci` has `-m "<message>"` | Using Subversion › Examples › Starting a New Plugin |
| DEV-134 | "Remember your username is case sensitive." | Process — check: exact-case `--username` | Using Subversion › Examples › Starting a New Plugin |
| DEV-135 | "Each time you make a formal release of your plugin, you should tag a copy of that release's code." | Every `Version:` in readme changelog has a tag | Using Subversion › Examples › "Tagging" New Versions |
| DEV-136 | "For the sake of the WordPress.org plugin browser, the new subdirectory should always look like a version number. `2.0.1.3` is good. `Cool hotness tag` is bad." | `svn ls tags/` entries all match `^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)*$` | Using Subversion › Examples › "Tagging" New Versions |
| DEV-137 | "We want to use `svn cp` instead of the regular `cp` in order to take advantage of SVN's features." | Process — check: tags made with `svn cp`, not filesystem copy + `svn add` | Using Subversion › Examples › "Tagging" New Versions |
| DEV-138 | "When tagging a new version, remember to update the `Stable Tag` field in `trunk/readme.txt`  to the new version." | `trunk/readme.txt` `Stable tag:` = newest tag folder name | Using Subversion › Examples › "Tagging" New Versions |
| DEV-139 | "Don't put anything in SVN that you're not willing and prepared to have deployed to everyone who uses your plugin. This includes vendor files, `.gitignore` and everything else." | Package/trunk contains no `.git/`, `.gitignore`, `.github/`, `node_modules/`, `tests/`, `phpunit.xml`, `.env`, `.DS_Store`, `composer.lock` unless intended; `vendor/` only if shipped on purpose | Using Subversion › Notes |
| DEV-140 | "You also should never upload zip files." | No `*.zip` anywhere in trunk/tags/package | Using Subversion › Notes |

## block-specific-plugin-guidelines.txt — Block Specific Plugin Guidelines
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://developer.wordpress.org/plugins/wordpress-org/block-specific-plugin-guidelines/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-141 | "All Block Specific plugins must also comply with the overall plugin guidelines ." | Block plugins also run the full Detailed Guidelines audit | Block Guidelines › intro |
| DEV-142 | "Users will have a place they can go … where they can download and install blocks that have been pre-vetted for major security issues." | Block code passes the same security greps (`$_POST` without `sanitize_`, unescaped output in `render_callback`/`render.php`) | Block Guidelines › User Expectations |
| DEV-143 | "Users will be able to one-click install blocks from their admin, one at a time." | Block works after one-click install with no setup step | Block Guidelines › User Expectations |
| DEV-144 | "Blocks will appear in their block library after installation and activation." | `register_block_type(` runs on `init` with the `block.json` path; block appears in inserter | Block Guidelines › User Expectations |
| DEV-145 | "Blocks will work seamlessly and immediately without intrusive advertisements or upselling." | No upsell/ad strings ("Pro", "Upgrade", "Buy") in block JS/PHP | Block Guidelines › User Expectations |
| DEV-146 | "Blocks submitted to the Block Directory are held to stricter technical and policy rules than WordPress plugins in general." | Apply all rows below in addition to general guideline rows | Block Guidelines › Developer Expectations |
| DEV-147 | "Plugins with blocks that do not meet the Block Directory Guidelines may still be submitted to the Plugin Directory." | Decide: Block Directory listing vs ordinary Plugin Directory | Block Guidelines › Developer Expectations |
| DEV-148 | "These guidelines apply specifically to the first type of plugin, which is called a Block Plugin. If a plugin is of the second type, then the further guidelines and restrictions do not apply." | Classify: plugin exists solely to distribute one block → rows below apply; otherwise skip them | Block Guidelines › Definitions |
| DEV-149 | "All plugins, be they block-only or not, are required to comply with the Detailed Plugin Guidelines ." | Full Detailed Guidelines audit for every plugin | Block Guidelines › Definitions |
| DEV-150 | "The Block Directory contains only Block Plugins, that is to say plugins that contain only a single, independent, top-level block and a minimum of supporting code." | Count `block.json` files / `registerBlockType(` calls: one top-level block | Block Guidelines › Block Plugins and the Block Directory |
| DEV-151 | "A specific type of WordPress plugin, with the same structure including a `readme.txt` file." | `readme.txt` present with standard headers | Block Guidelines › Block Plugins and the Block Directory |
| DEV-152 | "Containing only blocks (typically a single top-level block)." | No non-block features (shortcodes, widgets, CPTs, cron) | Block Guidelines › Block Plugins and the Block Directory |
| DEV-153 | "Contain a minimum of server-side (i.e. PHP) code." | PHP limited to main file + `register_block_type(` (+ optional `render.php`) | Block Guidelines › Block Plugins and the Block Directory |
| DEV-154 | "Self-contained with no UI outside of the post editor." | No `add_menu_page(`, `add_options_page(`, `add_submenu_page(`, `add_settings_section(` | Block Guidelines › Block Plugins and the Block Directory |
| DEV-155 | "A Block Plugin must contain a block, and a minimum of other supporting code." | At least one `block.json` + `register_block_type(` | Block Guidelines › 1. Block Plugins are for the Block Editor |
| DEV-156 | "It may not contain any UX outside of the editor, such as WordPress options or `wp-admin` menus." | No `register_setting(`, `add_menu_page(`, `add_options_page(`, admin notices, settings links | Block Guidelines › 1. Block Plugins are for the Block Editor |
| DEV-157 | "Server-side code should be kept to a minimum." | Small PHP footprint; count PHP files/lines | Block Guidelines › 1. Block Plugins are for the Block Editor |
| DEV-158 | "Plugins that only extend or provide styles for other, pre-existing blocks are currently not eligible for inclusion in the Block Directory." | Not eligible if only `registerBlockStyle(`, `registerBlockVariation(` or filters on `core/*` blocks | Block Guidelines › 1. Block Plugins are for the Block Editor |
| DEV-159 | "Only Block Plugins that register a new block are currently supported." | `block.json` `name` is a new, own-namespace block | Block Guidelines › 1. Block Plugins are for the Block Editor |
| DEV-160 | "Block plugins are intended to be single purpose, separate, independent blocks, not bundles or compendiums of many blocks." | One purpose; not a block library | Block Guidelines › 2. Block Plugins are separate blocks |
| DEV-161 | "In most cases, a Block Plugin should contain only one single top-level block." | Only one `block.json` without `parent`/`ancestor` | Block Guidelines › 2. Block Plugins are separate blocks |
| DEV-162 | "The Block Directory will not include collections of blocks that could be reasonably split up into separate, independent, blocks." | Multiple independent top-level `block.json` files → split or not eligible | Block Guidelines › 2. Block Plugins are separate blocks |
| DEV-163 | "Block plugins may contain more than one block where a clearly necessary parent/child or container/content dependency exists" | Extra blocks declare `"parent"` or `"ancestor"` in `block.json` pointing to the main block | Block Guidelines › 2. Block Plugins are separate blocks |
| DEV-164 | "Plugin titles and block titles should describe what the block does in a way that helps users easily understand its purpose." | `Plugin Name:` and `block.json` `title` describe the function | Block Guidelines › 3. Plugin and block names should reflect the block's purpose |
| DEV-165 | "In most cases the plugin title and the block title should be identical or very similar." | Compare `Plugin Name:` with `block.json` `title` | Block Guidelines › 3. Plugin and block names should reflect the block's purpose |
| DEV-166 | "Please avoid plugin and block titles unrelated to the block itself, or that cannot be easily distinguished from core blocks." | `title` not a company name only, nor a core block name ("Image", "Widget") | Block Guidelines › 3. Plugin and block names should reflect the block's purpose |
| DEV-167 | "The same trademark restrictions for plugin titles exist for block titles and names." | `block.json` `title`/`name` contain no third-party trademark unless by its owner | Block Guidelines › 3. Plugin and block names should reflect the block's purpose |
| DEV-168 | "The block name (meaning the `name` parameter to `registerBlockType()`  and `name` in `block.json` ) must be unique to the block." | `block.json` `name` and `registerBlockType(` name are unique (search directory for collisions) | Block Guidelines › 3a. Block names should be unique and properly namespaced |
| DEV-169 | "As with titles, please respect trademarks and other projects' commonly used names, so as not to conflict with them." | Namespace/name not another project's common prefix (e.g. `woocommerce/`, `jetpack/`) | Block Guidelines › 3a. Block names should be unique and properly namespaced |
| DEV-170 | "The namespace prefix to the block name should reflect either the plugin author, or the plugin slug." | `name` prefix before `/` = plugin slug or author handle | Block Guidelines › 3a. Block names should be unique and properly namespaced |
| DEV-171 | "The namespace may not be a reserved one such as `core` or `wordpress`." | `name` does not start with `core/` or `wordpress/` | Block Guidelines › 3a. Block names should be unique and properly namespaced |
| DEV-172 | "Block Plugins must include a valid `block.json` file." | `block.json` exists and validates against the block-metadata schema (`"$schema": "https://schemas.wp.org/trunk/block.json"`) | Block Guidelines › 4. Block Plugins must include a block.json file |
| DEV-173 | "the `block.json` must include the following attributes: … `name`" | `block.json` has `"name"` | Block Guidelines › 4. Block Plugins must include a block.json file |
| DEV-174 | "`title`" | `block.json` has `"title"` | Block Guidelines › 4. Block Plugins must include a block.json file |
| DEV-175 | "At least one of: `script`, `editorScript`" | `block.json` has `"script"` or `"editorScript"` | Block Guidelines › 4. Block Plugins must include a block.json file |
| DEV-176 | "At least one of: `style`, `editorStyle`" | `block.json` has `"style"` or `"editorStyle"` | Block Guidelines › 4. Block Plugins must include a block.json file |
| DEV-177 | "Block Plugins must function by themselves without requiring any external dependencies such as another WordPress plugin or theme." | No `Requires Plugins:` header; no fatal when other plugins absent (`class_exists(`/`function_exists(` guards) | Block Guidelines › 5. Block Plugins must work independently |
| DEV-178 | "A Block Plugin may make use of code or hooks in another WordPress plugin or theme, provided the Block Plugin is still usable without that plugin or theme installed." | Third-party hooks/functions wrapped in `function_exists(`/`has_filter(` checks | Block Guidelines › 5. Block Plugins must work independently |
| DEV-179 | "they should not encumber the user with additional steps or prerequisites such as installing another plugin or theme, signing up for an account, or logging in or manually connecting to an external service." | No API-key fields, sign-up prompts, OAuth connect flows, or "install X plugin" notices | Block Guidelines › 6. Block Plugins should work seamlessly |
| DEV-180 | "No form of payment is permitted for the use of a Block Plugin." | No license-key checks, paywalls, `checkout`/`purchase` flows in block code | Block Guidelines › 6. Block Plugins should work seamlessly |
| DEV-181 | "While it is allowed to use the donation link feature in the plugin's readme, or to link from the full plugin listing, the Block Plugin itself must be free to use." | Readme `Donate link:` allowed; block UI has no payment links | Block Guidelines › 6. Block Plugins should work seamlessly |
| DEV-182 | "Block plugins that do require a paid service or include upselling and premium features are still permitted in the main WordPress Plugin Directory, subject to its guidelines." | If paid service/upsell exists → Plugin Directory only, not Block Directory | Block Guidelines › 6. Block Plugins should work seamlessly |
| DEV-183 | "Block Plugins may use an external/cloud API where necessary, provided it can be done seamlessly without requiring a login or activation key." | `fetch(`/`wp_remote_get(`/`apiFetch(` to external hosts carry no user-supplied key or login | Block Guidelines › 6. Block Plugins should work seamlessly |
| DEV-184 | "They should not rely on an external API or cloud service for functions that could be performed locally." | Each external call (`wp_remote_`, `fetch(` to non-site URLs) justified; no remote call for local work | Block Guidelines › 6. Block Plugins should work seamlessly |
| DEV-185 | "As much as possible, they should avoid including additional PHP code or server-side libraries." | No `vendor/` PHP libraries; minimal PHP | Block Guidelines › 7. Server-side code should be kept to a minimum |
| DEV-186 | "The WordPress REST API should be the preferred interface to WordPress, rather than custom server-side code." | Prefer `@wordpress/api-fetch` against core endpoints over custom `register_rest_route(`/`wp_ajax_` | Block Guidelines › 7. Server-side code should be kept to a minimum |
| DEV-187 | "In those situations, PHP code may be included, provided it is clearly written, used as little as possible, and well documented." | Any extra PHP is short and has docblocks explaining why | Block Guidelines › 7. Server-side code should be kept to a minimum |

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-189 | "Plugins that have been approved but no code has yet been uploaded will see this message:This only displays to the plugin owner and will go away once code has been pushed via SVN." | Process — check: push trunk + readme + first tag soon after approval | Alerts and Warnings › Approved and Pending Data |
| DEV-190 | "As of November 2017, plugins that are closed display a notice: … This is viewable by all visitors and indicates a plugin was closed." | Process — check: closure is public | Alerts and Warnings › Closed |
| DEV-191 | "After 60 days, the alert will be updated to explain why the plugin was closed" | Process — check: closure reason becomes public after 60 days | Alerts and Warnings › Closed |
| DEV-192 | "Author Request – the author has asked the plugin to be closed" | Process — check: closure reason category | Alerts and Warnings › Reasons why plugins are closed |
| DEV-193 | "Guideline Violation – a violation of any of the guideline" | Full Detailed Guidelines audit before every release | Alerts and Warnings › Reasons why plugins are closed |
| DEV-194 | "Licensing/Trademark Violation – non-GPL code in use, or trademarks are being misused" | `License:` GPL-compatible; every bundled library (`vendor/`, `assets/js/*`, fonts) has a GPL-compatible licence; no third-party trademark at the start of `Plugin Name:`/slug | Alerts and Warnings › Reasons why plugins are closed |
| DEV-195 | "Merged Into Core – the plugin is now a part of core (reserved for feature projects)" | Process — check: not applicable to non-feature projects | Alerts and Warnings › Reasons why plugins are closed |
| DEV-196 | "Security Issue – a security concern has been found in this plugin" | Security greps: unsanitized `$_GET`/`$_POST`/`$_REQUEST`, unescaped `echo`, missing nonces/`current_user_can(`, `$wpdb` without `prepare(` | Alerts and Warnings › Reasons why plugins are closed |
| DEV-197 | "Additional details on why a plugin is closed are not provided to anyone outside the WordPress.org security team or the plugin authors, unless there is an extreme circumstance." | Process — check: closure details only via the authors' email | Alerts and Warnings › Reasons why plugins are closed |
| DEV-198 | "Plugins that do not support the last 3 major releases of WordPress have the following notice:" | Readme `Tested up to:` is within the last 3 major WordPress releases | Alerts and Warnings › Out of Date |
| DEV-199 | "this alert can be avoided by updating a plugin readme when new versions of WordPress is released." | Process — check: bump `Tested up to:` at each WordPress major | Alerts and Warnings › Out of Date |
| DEV-200 | "They do not need to push a new version, just update the readme  and edit the value of `Tested up to:` to the latest version of WordPress." | `Tested up to:` in readme of `trunk` and of the stable tag = latest WP major | Alerts and Warnings › Out of Date |
