## hb-expectations.txt — Expectations
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/expectations/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-1 | "Everyone is expected to be familiar with the Detailed Plugin Guidelines . Those guidelines must be enforced as consistently and fairly as possible" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: every one of the Detailed Plugin Guidelines is applied literally; audit against each guideline before upload, "other plugins do it" carries no weight. | Expectations › Education |
| HB-2 | "We cannot tell anyone other than the plugin owner(s) why a plugin was closed." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: closure/rejection reasons arrive only at the owner account's email; keep that inbox monitored (and whitelisted). | Expectations › Privileged Information |
| HB-3 | "You have to remain respectful no matter what." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: keep review correspondence civil; abuse of reviewers is itself a violation (see Behavioral Violations rows). | Expectations › Respect |
| HB-4 | "We don't offer training in how to code, but at the same time we try to guide people to information and allow them to self-educate." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a review email names examples and links docs; find and fix every instance across the codebase, not only the quoted lines. | Expectations › Communication |
| HB-5 | "some people have been kicked out of the repository for behavior – such as name-calling, constant SEO abuse, or flagrant disrespect" | Readme `Tags:` and description for keyword stuffing / SEO spam; tone of support replies and review emails. Behaviour can close all plugins of an account. | Expectations › Communication |
| HB-6 | "When a plugin is told "you may not include your own copy of jquery" we get roughly the same amount of grief" | Bundled `*jquery*.js` files in the ZIP; `wp_deregister_script( 'jquery'`; `wp_enqueue_script(` with a jQuery `src`; use the core handle `'jquery'` as a dependency instead. | Expectations › On Saying No |
| HB-7 | "being firm and telling people "no" – and not bending – is part of the role." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a reviewer's "no" is final; comply in the first reply rather than argue for an exception. | Expectations › On Saying No |
| HB-8 | "There will always be exceptions (like the "Use Google's jQuery" plugin which has the sole purpose of using Google's jQuery), but that one makes logical sense." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: an exception exists only when the forbidden thing is the plugin's sole purpose; a media plugin gets no remote/bundled-jQuery exception. | Expectations › On Saying No |

## hb-performing-reviews.txt — Performing Reviews
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/performing-reviews/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-9 | "The steps taken to review a new plugin are actually the same as those for reviewing any existing plugin." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: every resubmission, update review or reopening gets the full new-plugin checklist, not a diff review. | Performing Reviews › Overview |
| HB-10 | "The goal is to look for as much 'wrong' as possible, identify it clearly, and communicate that back to the developer." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: self-audit the whole ZIP (all files, vendor dirs included) before upload; reviewers hunt, they don't sample. | Performing Reviews › Overview |
| HB-11 | "A plugin author is vetted to ensure they are not currently violating guidelines." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: the account's other plugins, forum standing and past rejections are checked before the code. | Performing Reviews › Overview |
| HB-12 | "The plugin is downloaded and reviewed for subject matter, security, and quality." | Subject: readme short description + `== Description ==`; security: `$_POST`/`$_GET`/`$_REQUEST` handling, `esc_*`, nonces, `current_user_can(`; quality: notices under `WP_DEBUG`. | Performing Reviews › Overview |
| HB-13 | "The plugin is moved to 'pending', and an email is sent to the author with information re what to correct." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: "pending" means the next move is yours: fix and reply. | Performing Reviews › Overview |
| HB-14 | "The author replies with the corrected code and the plugin is approved, or the email cycle continues (return to step 4)." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: upload the corrected ZIP and reply to the email; each round re-runs the whole review. | Performing Reviews › Overview |

## hb-performing-reviews_review-admin-tool.txt — Using the Review Admin Tool
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/performing-reviews/review-admin-tool/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-15 | "Most important at this stage is the ZIP file. Use that to download the new ZIP for your review." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: only the uploaded ZIP is judged; build it from a clean tree (no `.git`, `.env`, `node_modules`, test fixtures, build caches) and include unminified sources. | Using the Review Admin Tool |
| HB-16 | "Perform the steps on the Review Walkthrough  and Review Checklist ." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: the Checklist and Walkthrough rows in this file are the self-audit to run before upload. | Using the Review Admin Tool |
| HB-17 | "Calls `wp-load.php` on multiple pages." | `wp-load.php`, `wp-config.php`, `wp-blog-header.php`, `require.*ABSPATH`-less bootstraps in standalone PHP endpoints; use `admin-ajax.php` / `register_rest_route` instead. | Using the Review Admin Tool › example note |
| HB-18 | "Saves files to plugin directory." | `file_put_contents(`, `fopen(`, `fwrite(`, `move_uploaded_file(`, `copy(`, `$wp_filesystem->put_contents(` with targets built from `plugin_dir_path(`, `__DIR__`, `dirname( __FILE__ )`, `WP_PLUGIN_DIR`. | Using the Review Admin Tool › example note |
| HB-19 | "Includes own jQuery." | Bundled `*jquery*.js` (also jquery-ui, jquery.min.js) in the ZIP; enqueue of a local or remote jQuery file instead of the core `'jquery'` handle. | Using the Review Admin Tool › example note |
| HB-20 | "Calls bootstrap CSS… externally from a CDN." | `wp_enqueue_style(`/`wp_enqueue_script(`/`<link`/`<script src=` pointing at `cdn.`, `bootstrapcdn`, `cdnjs`, `jsdelivr`, `unpkg`, `googleapis.com/ajax`. | Using the Review Admin Tool › example note |
| HB-21 | "Author has `wordpress` in their domain name." | Plugin header `Plugin URI:`, `Author URI:`, readme links and `Donate link:` — domains containing "wordpress" are flagged (use "wp"). | Using the Review Admin Tool › example note |
| HB-22 | "Setting a plugin to pending will not email the developer automatically." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a status change in the dashboard is not a notification; the explanation arrives as a separate Help Scout email. | Using the Review Admin Tool |
| HB-23 | "Rejected per author request." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a submission can be withdrawn by asking; it is then rejected, not left pending. | Using the Review Admin Tool › rejection examples |
| HB-24 | "Duplicate submission." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: never submit the same plugin twice (new slug/account) while one is pending; it is rejected outright. | Using the Review Admin Tool › rejection examples |
| HB-25 | "Author banned from directory." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a banned account's new submissions are rejected without review. | Using the Review Admin Tool › rejection examples |
| HB-26 | "All pending plugins will remain in this state until they can be approved." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: there is no time-out approval; a pending plugin waits on the developer's fix. | Using the Review Admin Tool |

## hb-performing-reviews_review-checklist.txt — Review Checklist
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/performing-reviews/review-checklist/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-27 | "Verify the submitted name and subject matter are acceptable." | Plugin header `Plugin Name:`, readme `=== Name ===`, slug (folder name): first term not someone else's trademark, no `wordpress`/`plugin`; readme description states an acceptable subject. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-28 | "Download the ZIP." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: the ZIP as uploaded is the artifact; check its file list (`unzip -l`) for stray dev files. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-29 | "Check all files for guideline violations." | Every file in the ZIP incl. `vendor/`, `lib/`, `assets/`, `build/`, `languages/`, `.js` bundles — not just the main PHP file. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-30 | "Ensure the `readme` is clear (only a requirement for services )." | readme.txt `== Description ==`, `== Installation ==`, `== Frequently Asked Questions ==`: a service plugin must say what the service is and how to sign up. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-31 | "Test in a secure environment." | Install the ZIP in a fresh WordPress with `WP_DEBUG` on: activate, use every screen, deactivate, uninstall — no fatals, notices or outgoing calls before consent. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-32 | "Detail any issues found and email the developer from HelpScout…" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: issues arrive by email from the review team; reply in that thread. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-33 | "If no issues are found, approve the plugin…" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: zero findings is the bar for approval. | Review Checklist › Reviewer Workflow |
| HB-34 | "All plugins and developers are required to comply with all Plugin Directory Guidelines as well as the Forum Guidelines  and WordCamp Code of Conducts  (when applicable)." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: forum/support behaviour counts toward the plugin's standing as well as code. | Review Checklist › Required |
| HB-35 | "100% adherence to the Detailed Plugin Guidelines ." | Run the full guideline audit (all 18 guidelines) against the ZIP and readme; any single miss pends the review. | Review Checklist › Required |
| HB-36 | "Full support of the current version of WordPress." | readme `Tested up to:` = current major; no calls to functions removed/deprecated in current WP (`_deprecated_function` notices under `WP_DEBUG`); plugin header `Requires at least:`. | Review Checklist › Required |
| HB-37 | "The plugin cannot be a 100% copy of another plugin. Forks are permitted, however they must show significant improvements or changes to the original." | For a fork: diff against the original's last release; readme must describe what is new/changed; identical files with only renamed prefixes are flagged. | Review Checklist › Required |
| HB-38 | "The plugin must be the developer's own work. Submission of another person's plugin is not permitted." | Plugin header `Author:`/`Author URI:` and readme `Contributors:` match the submitting account; no third-party plugin re-uploaded as own. | Review Checklist › Required |
| HB-39 | "If included in the review, the plugin header… image and logos must be family friendly and not be offensive." | `assets/banner-*.png`, `assets/icon-*.png`, screenshots, logos in `images/` / `assets/`. | Review Checklist › Required |
| HB-40 | "Black or grey hat SEO (including plugins that auto-post content and content spinners)." | Code that creates posts automatically (`wp_insert_post(` on cron/import without user action), text "spinning"/rewriting features; readme claims about SEO. | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-41 | "Plugins that state to 'help you earn thousands of dollars' or other improbable claims." | readme short description, `== Description ==`, admin screen copy: income/ranking/performance promises ("guaranteed", "10x", "earn"). | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-42 | "Frameworks, boilerplates, and libraries plugins." | A plugin that does nothing on its own and only exposes APIs/classes for other plugins; readme describing it as a "framework"/"library". | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-43 | "Plugins that require themes or plugins to be edited for use." | readme/Installation steps telling users to edit `functions.php`, theme templates or another plugin's files; code that writes into `get_template_directory()` / other plugins' dirs. | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-44 | "Marketplace or storefront only plugins." | A plugin whose only function is listing/selling products or add-ons (admin page that is just a shop/upsell catalogue). | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-45 | "Plugins that reproduce core WordPress functions or features without perceivable improvements (for example, a plugin that allows embedding YouTube videos)." | Features duplicating core: oEmbed/`[embed]`, `[gallery]`, `[audio]`/`[video]`, media upload, RSS widget; readme must show the added value. | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-46 | "Plugins that allow users to paste in raw JS/CSS…/HTML…/PHP…  without sanitization or security." | Settings fields saved raw and echoed (`update_option(` of textarea then `echo get_option(` unescaped), `eval(`, `create_function(`, `include` of user-supplied paths, `wp_unslash` without `wp_kses`/`sanitize_*`; no `current_user_can( 'unfiltered_html' )` gate. | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-47 | "Please note: All existing plugins in the directory are permitted to remain, and will not be deleted unless there are extreme circumstances." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: existing plugins doing a forbidden thing are no precedent for a new submission. | Review Checklist › Subject Matter |
| HB-48 | "Be 100% GPLv2 or later and/or 100% GPL…-compatible licensed." | Plugin header `License:` / `License URI:`, readme `License:`; every bundled file/library's licence (`LICENSE`, `composer.json` `license`, `package.json` `license`, JS file headers) GPL-compatible. | Review Checklist › Licensing |
| HB-49 | "Copyright and licenses must be explicitly declared using the license and License URI header slugs in the `readme`." | readme.txt header lines `License: GPLv2 or later` and `License URI: https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-2.0.html`. | Review Checklist › Licensing |
| HB-50 | "Licenses of any resources included such as fonts or images must be declared in the plugin header." | Bundled fonts (`*.woff*`, `*.ttf`), icon sets, images: licence + source stated (plugin header / readme / a credits file). | Review Checklist › Licensing |
| HB-51 | "Code and design should be original or legally permitted for use." | Copied code blocks/CSS/icons from commercial products or themes; header comments of third-party files. | Review Checklist › Licensing |
| HB-52 | "Forks must be appropriately credited; no copyright information may be removed." | Fork: original authors' copyright lines kept in file headers and LICENSE; readme credits the original plugin and author. | Review Checklist › Licensing |
| HB-53 | "Information on how to configure the plugin is recommended." | readme `== Installation ==` / `== Frequently Asked Questions ==` covering settings location and setup. | Review Checklist › Readme |
| HB-54 | "If 3rd party services are used, the `readme` must… Clearly disclose as to what is used, when, and why." | Every `wp_remote_get(`/`wp_remote_post(`/`wp_remote_request(`/`wp_safe_remote_*`/`fetch(`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`curl_` host listed in readme (e.g. an `== External services ==` section): which service, what data, when sent, why. | Review Checklist › Readme |
| HB-55 | "Include links to the service's terms of use and/or privacy policy." | readme service section: a terms-of-use URL and a privacy-policy URL for each external host. | Review Checklist › Readme |
| HB-56 | "If no support is provided, the `readme` must indicate such in clear terms." | readme statement on support (or explicit "no support provided"). | Review Checklist › Readme |
| HB-57 | "No PHP or JS errors." | `php -l` on every file; browser console on every admin screen; Plugin Check errors. | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-58 | "No errors when run with `WP_DEBUG` set to true." | Run with `define( 'WP_DEBUG', true )`: undefined index/variable notices, deprecated notices, `_doing_it_wrong` (e.g. enqueue before `wp_enqueue_scripts`). | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-59 | "Recommended plugins: Developer , Debug Bar , Query Monitor ." | Test with Query Monitor active: PHP errors, doing-it-wrong, HTTP API calls panel (outgoing requests on page load). | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-60 | "Validation, sanitization, and escaping of all processed or saved data." | `$_POST`/`$_GET`/`$_REQUEST`/`$_COOKIE`/`$_SERVER`/`$_FILES` without `sanitize_*`/`absint`/`wp_unslash`; `echo`/`print`/`printf` without `esc_html`/`esc_attr`/`esc_url`/`wp_kses`; REST args without `sanitize_callback`/`validate_callback`. | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-61 | "Use of a unique prefix for everything the plugin defines in the public namespace (options, functions, global variables, constants, post meta…, etc.)." | `function ` names, `class `, `define(`, `const `, `add_option(`/`update_option(` keys, `update_post_meta(` keys, `register_setting(`, `wp_enqueue_*` handles, `add_shortcode(`, AJAX `wp_ajax_` actions, `register_rest_route(` namespace, transients, `global $` — all carry the plugin prefix (or a namespace). | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-62 | "Valid `readme`." | readme.txt parses: `=== Name ===`, `Contributors:`, `Tags:`, `Requires at least:`, `Tested up to:`, `Stable tag:` (matches plugin header `Version:`), `License:`, short description ≤150 chars; run the readme validator. | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-63 | "No saving content locally to the plugin folder, as it is deleted on upgrades." | Writes to `plugin_dir_path( __FILE__ )`, `__DIR__`, `WP_PLUGIN_DIR` (`file_put_contents(`, `fopen(`, `mkdir(`, logs, caches); use `wp_upload_dir()`. | Review Checklist › Code |
| HB-64 | "Using WordPress functionality and features first." | Hand-rolled HTTP (`curl_init(`, `file_get_contents('http`), raw SQL where a WP API exists, own upload handling instead of `media_handle_upload(`/`wp_handle_upload(`, own JSON endpoints instead of REST API. | Review Checklist › Core Functionality and Features |
| HB-65 | "Using WordPress content directory functions  to determine locations of folders and files." | Hard-coded `wp-content`, `/wp-content/plugins/`, `ABSPATH . 'wp-content'`, `site_url() . '/wp-content'`; use `plugin_dir_path(`, `plugins_url(`, `plugin_dir_url(`, `content_url(`, `wp_upload_dir(`, `WP_CONTENT_DIR`. | Review Checklist › Core Functionality and Features |
| HB-66 | "Avoiding hard coding to modify content (using function parameters, filters and action hooks… where appropriate)." | Output-buffer rewriting (`ob_start(` callbacks on whole page), string-replacing core markup, editing post content in DB instead of `the_content`/other filters. | Review Checklist › Core Functionality and Features |
| HB-67 | "Avoiding duplication of existing WordPress core features (for example, embedding YouTube)." | Features that re-implement core media/embeds/shortcodes; readme must state the improvement over core. | Review Checklist › Core Functionality and Features |
| HB-68 | "Tags and descriptions matching what the plugin does and what it connects with." | readme `Tags:` (accurate, no competitor/brand names unrelated to the integration), short description and `== Description ==` match actual features and services contacted. | Review Checklist › Core Functionality and Features |
| HB-69 | "Requirement checks fail gracefully when not present." | `class_exists(`/`function_exists(`/`is_plugin_active(`/`version_compare( PHP_VERSION` checks before use; on failure an `admin_notices` message and early return, not a fatal or `deactivate_plugins(` of other plugins. | Review Checklist › Core Functionality and Features |
| HB-70 | "Custom features, options or any limitations (for example, menu restrictions), should be explained contextually and within the `readme`." | Settings screens carry help text for each option; readme FAQ/Description documents options and limits (e.g. free vs Pro limits, file-size caps). | Review Checklist › Documentation |
| HB-71 | "Any remote calls (such as serviceware calling it's own servers to process spam) must be disclosed in the `readme`." | Inventory every remote host (`wp_remote_`, `wp_safe_remote_`, `download_url(`, `fetch(`, `wp_oembed_get(`, update checkers like `pre_set_site_transient_update_plugins`) against the readme disclosure. | Review Checklist › Documentation |
| HB-72 | "Any external requirements such as registration with a service must be documented in the `readme` and the Settings page." | API-key/account fields on the settings page explain where/how to register; same instructions in readme Installation/FAQ. | Review Checklist › Documentation |
| HB-73 | "Don't include any themes. A theme can be required but not included or auto-installed." | `style.css` with `Theme Name:` in the ZIP; `Theme_Upgrader`, `switch_theme(`, theme ZIPs. | Review Checklist › Themes and other Plugins |
| HB-74 | "Don't include other plugins wholesale. A plugin can be required but not included or auto-installed." | Other plugins' folders/ZIPs bundled; `Plugin_Upgrader`, `activate_plugin(`, `install_plugin_install_status` used to auto-install; files with a second `Plugin Name:` header. | Review Checklist › Themes and other Plugins |
| HB-75 | "Don't do things in a plugin considered theme territory (exception: mobile plugins may include mobile themes)." | Front-end template overrides (`template_include`, `template_redirect` replacing whole pages), global front-end restyling of the theme. | Review Checklist › Themes and other Plugins |
| HB-76 | "Do not require other themes or plugins be edited for use as those changes would be erased on updates." | readme/instructions asking to paste code into theme or other plugins; code writing into `get_stylesheet_directory()` or `WP_PLUGIN_DIR/<other-plugin>`. | Review Checklist › Themes and other Plugins |
| HB-77 | "Don't phone home… without informed user consent." | Any `wp_remote_*`/`fetch(` on activation, `admin_init`, cron or page load sending site URL, email, IP, usage — must sit behind an explicit opt-in or a user action. | Review Checklist › Security and Privacy |
| HB-78 | "Collection of user data must be "opt-in" only and have the relevant option set to disabled by default." | Default values in `add_option(`/`register_setting( … 'default'` for telemetry/tracking/analytics options = off; no data sent before the opt-in is saved. | Review Checklist › Security and Privacy |
| HB-79 | "Validate and sanitize untrusted data before processing (See: Data Validation )." | Superglobals, REST `$request` params, `$_FILES`, remote API responses passed to SQL/filesystem/options without `sanitize_*`, `absint`, `sanitize_file_name`, `wp_check_filetype_and_ext`. | Review Checklist › Security and Privacy |
| HB-80 | "Escape all data before output (See: Data Validation )." | `echo`/`print`/`printf`/`?>…<?php echo` and JS-inlined data without `esc_html(`, `esc_attr(`, `esc_url(`, `esc_js(`, `wp_kses_post(`, `wp_json_encode(`; `_e(`/`__(` echoed raw (use `esc_html_e(`). | Review Checklist › Security and Privacy |
| HB-81 | "Do not use URL… shorteners." | Links in code/readme to `bit.ly`, `goo.gl`, `t.co`, `tinyurl`, `ow.ly`, custom short domains. | Review Checklist › Security and Privacy |
| HB-82 | "Use `prepare()` and `$wpdb` for SQL calls." | `$wpdb->query(`/`get_results(`/`get_var(`/`get_row(`/`get_col(` with interpolated variables and no `$wpdb->prepare(`; `mysqli_`/`mysql_`/`PDO` direct use. | Review Checklist › Security and Privacy |
| HB-83 | "Upselling is permitted from plugin settings screen or a link on their entry on the plugin list page." | Upsell/Pro notices outside the plugin's own settings page and `plugin_action_links_`/`plugin_row_meta` — e.g. site-wide `admin_notices`, dashboard widgets, other plugins' screens. | Review Checklist › Selling, credits, and links |
| HB-84 | "Forward facing links (including credit links, powered by, and ads) must be optional and not active by default." | Front-end output (`wp_footer`, `the_content`, shortcodes, widgets) adding "Powered by"/credit links; option default must be off. | Review Checklist › Selling, credits, and links |
| HB-85 | "Sponsored links are permitted within reason." | Sponsored/partner links in admin screens or readme: few and relevant. | Review Checklist › Selling, credits, and links |
| HB-86 | "Third Party Ads are not permitted due to tracking." | Ad network scripts/iframes (`googlesyndication`, `adsbygoogle`, ad iframes) in admin or front end. | Review Checklist › Selling, credits, and links |
| HB-87 | "Affiliate links should be avoided wherever possible, and unhidden when used — use the real link to the affiliate, not a custom shortened URL." | Links with `ref=`, `aff=`, `affiliate`, redirect/cloaked domains in readme and admin screens. | Review Checklist › Selling, credits, and links |
| HB-88 | "UTM links to a developer's site are allowed anywhere links are permitted." | `utm_source=` links pointing at the developer's own domain — OK; to third parties only under the affiliate/sponsored rules. | Review Checklist › Selling, credits, and links |
| HB-89 | "No hard coding of scripts or styles; use `wp_enqueue_*()`." | Literal `<script`, `<script src=`, `<link rel="stylesheet"`, `<style>` blocks echoed in PHP; use `wp_enqueue_script(`/`wp_enqueue_style(`/`wp_add_inline_script(`/`wp_add_inline_style(`/`wp_localize_script(`. | Review Checklist › Stylesheets and Scripts |
| HB-90 | "No analytics or tracking by third parties." | `google-analytics.com`, `gtag(`, `googletagmanager`, `analytics.js`, Hotjar, Mixpanel, Segment, Sentry browser SDK, pixels in admin or front end. | Review Checklist › Stylesheets and Scripts |
| HB-91 | "No minification of scripts or files unless the original files are also provided." | Every `*.min.js`/`*.min.css`/bundled `build/*.js` has its source in the ZIP (or a public repo link in readme) plus build instructions. | Review Checklist › Stylesheets and Scripts |
| HB-92 | "No minification of scripts that prevents them from being human readable (for example, do not use `p,a,c,k,e,r`)." | `eval(function(p,a,c,k,e,`, `base64_decode(` of code blobs, obfuscated JS/PHP, `str_rot13(`, `gzinflate(`. | Review Checklist › Stylesheets and Scripts |
| HB-93 | "Use core-bundled scripts (for example: jQuery)." | Bundled copies of jQuery, jQuery UI, Underscore, Backbone, React (`wp-element`), Moment, Masonry, imagesLoaded, ThickBox, Clipboard — use core handles. | Review Checklist › Stylesheets and Scripts |
| HB-94 | "Include all scripts and resources locally (Exception: fonts are permitted to be remote loaded, services may also remote load on a case by case basis)." | `wp_enqueue_*(` / `<img src=` / CSS `url(` pointing at remote hosts (CDNs, developer's domain) for JS, CSS, images. | Review Checklist › Stylesheets and Scripts |
| HB-95 | "Plugins should follow the Accessibility Handbook ." | Admin UI: form `<label for>`, focus states, keyboard operation, `aria-*` on custom widgets, colour contrast. | Review Checklist › Recommended |
| HB-96 | "Code should be written to support internationalization and automatic translations via translate.wordpress.org ." | All UI strings in `__(`/`_e(`/`esc_html__(`/`_n(` with the text domain = slug; plugin header `Text Domain:`; JS strings via `wp.i18n` + `wp_set_script_translations(`. | Review Checklist › Recommended |
| HB-97 | "Support of PHP 7.0.0 and up, or graceful failure if newer versions are required." | Plugin header `Requires PHP:`; syntax newer than declared (`match`, `?->`, enums, typed props); `version_compare( PHP_VERSION` guard with an admin notice. | Review Checklist › Recommended |
| HB-98 | "Proper alerts and errors if any required plugin or theme is not installed and active." | `admin_notices` when a dependency (`class_exists( 'WooCommerce' )` etc.) is missing; plugin header `Requires Plugins:`. | Review Checklist › Recommended |
| HB-99 | "Data transmission is secure and sanitized." | Remote endpoints use `https://` (no `http://` in `wp_remote_*` URLs), `sslverify` not set to `false`; outgoing payloads sanitized; API keys not in URLs. | Review Checklist › Serviceware Requirements |
| HB-100 | "Readme description (and FAQ) detail usage and registration." | readme `== Description ==` and `== Frequently Asked Questions ==` explain the service, sign-up/registration, pricing/credits. | Review Checklist › Serviceware Requirements |
| HB-101 | "Connectivity to the service is not performed via an iframe… in the dashboard (APIs are recommended)." | `<iframe` in admin pages loading the service's site; use `wp_remote_*` API calls instead. | Review Checklist › Serviceware Requirements |
| HB-102 | "Calling `wp-load` directly to gain access to core functions." | `wp-load.php`, `wp-config.php`, `wp-blog-header.php` in `require`/`include`; standalone PHP files reachable by URL. | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |
| HB-103 | "Trademark and/or Copyright violations. (There are certain exceptions for the owners of registered trademarks, some of which are listed here )." | Slug, `Plugin Name:`, banner/icon, readme title: third-party brand as first term; copied logos/assets. | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |
| HB-104 | "Remote loading data when not absolutely necessary." | Remote fetches of config, notices, banners, JSON "news" feeds, images (`wp_remote_get(` on `admin_init`/page load) that could ship locally. | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |
| HB-105 | "Terms of Use violations for 3rd party services (such as Yahoo's APIs and most finance related ones)." | Each external API used: its ToS permits this use (scraping, redistribution, commercial use, key sharing via a proxy). | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |
| HB-106 | "Tracking usage without explicit opt-in consent." | Usage/telemetry pings, activation/deactivation feedback calls, install counters sent without a stored opt-in flag. | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |
| HB-107 | "Using PHP Shorttags (for example, `<?=OPTION_NAME?>`)." | `<?=` and `<? ` (without `php`) in any PHP/template file. | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |
| HB-108 | "Non-GPLv2 (or later) compatible code." | Bundled code under proprietary, CC-NC, JSON licence ("Good, not Evil"), or no licence; `composer.json`/`package.json` licence fields of shipped deps. | Review Checklist › Not Permitted |

## hb-performing-reviews_review-walkthrough.txt — Review Walkthrough
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/performing-reviews/review-walkthrough/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-109 | "ensure the user is not currently prohibited from submitting new plugins, or attempting to evade restrictions due to a previous infraction." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: submit only from the account that owns the plugin; never a fresh account after a rejection. | Review Walkthrough › The User |
| HB-110 | "This should be considered a 'bad faith' action, and should be reported to a plugin review administrator immediately." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: new accounts or posing as other people to get around a 'no' is reported to admins as bad faith. | Review Walkthrough › The User |
| HB-111 | "If the plugin has been rejected before, or if the user has a large number of closed plugins, search for their email in HelpScout… and the Reviewer P2  to see why." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: earlier review rounds and closures are read before the new ZIP; every point raised before must be fixed. | Review Walkthrough › The User |
| HB-112 | "If the user is marked as being moderated in the forums… This, in and of itself, is not a reason to reject the plugin, but it must be considered." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: forum moderation status weighs in the review. | Review Walkthrough › The User |
| HB-113 | "We do not accept all plugins. We are not required to accept and host all plugins." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: acceptance is discretionary; the subject must be appropriate, not only the code clean. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-114 | "Reproduction of features included in WordPress, without any perceivable additions, such as (but not limited to) duplication of existing shortcodes… widgets (RSS feed… display), or functionality (adding users)." | `add_shortcode(` names that mirror core (`gallery`, `embed`, `audio`, `video`, `caption`), widgets re-implementing core widgets, features duplicating core admin functions. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-115 | "Duplication of work being done in feature projects, such as the JSON… API." | Features that re-create an active core feature project (e.g. own REST-like JSON API layer). | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-116 | "Storefronts." | Plugin whose main screen is a store/add-on catalogue. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-117 | "Intentionally unethical purposes, such as black or grey hat SEO (including auto posting or 'spinning' content)." | Auto-posting (`wp_insert_post(` on schedule from scraped/AI content), content spinners, link schemes. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-118 | "Impossible promises such as 'Our plugin will help you earn thousands of dollars'." | readme and admin copy: earnings, rankings, "guaranteed" results, unprovable percentages. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-119 | "Spamming (not spam blocking, actual spamming)." | Mass email/comment/post sending (`wp_mail(` loops to external lists, comment posting to other sites). | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-120 | "Creation of an unsafe Internet via spreading viruses or intentional security holes in WordPress." | Backdoors: hidden admin creation (`wp_insert_user(` / `wp_create_user(` with role administrator), `eval(`, `assert(`, `base64_decode(`, remote code fetch + `include`, disabled auth checks. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-121 | "Inspiration of serious threats of harm (if a plugin seems to only be created to harm people, it's probably not a good fit)." | Readme/subject review: purpose of the plugin. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-122 | "Plugin subjects that are illegal or incite violence/hate." | Readme/subject review: purpose and content of the plugin. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-123 | "We also do not accept plugins that are 100% copies of other plugins unless the intent is to takeover and enhance an existing but abandoned plugin that they were unable to adopt." | Fork: diff vs original; readme states the takeover/enhancement purpose and the changes; evidence of an adoption attempt (request to original authors / plugins team). | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-124 | "If there has been no attempt to adopt, the submission should be rejected and redirected." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: for a copy of an abandoned plugin, attempt adoption first and be able to show it (dates, ticket) in the review reply. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-125 | "we reserve the right to reject any plugin on any grounds we feel are reasonable, whether or not they are explicitly noted in the guidelines." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: passing the written guidelines is necessary but not sufficient. | Review Walkthrough › The Subject Matter |
| HB-126 | "Since plugin permalinks cannot be changed after approval you must make sure the defined permalink is as appropriate as possible before going further." | Slug (= ZIP folder name and main-file `Text Domain:`) chosen final before submit: no trademark first term, no `wordpress`/`plugin`, no typos. | Review Walkthrough › The Name |
| HB-127 | "While the Display Name can be changed at any time, we still check early to see if the name has any conflicts with existing trademarks." | Plugin header `Plugin Name:` and readme `=== Name ===`: no trademark conflicts. | Review Walkthrough › The Name |
| HB-128 | "Make sure the first term (or terms) isn't someone else's product." | First word(s) of slug/`Plugin Name:`: not WordPress, Drupal, Bootstrap, Font Awesome, WooCommerce or another product/plugin name (use "for <Product>" after your own name). | Review Walkthrough › Trademarks and Project Names |
| HB-129 | "You may need to read the code and check submitted URLs to make sure of ownership." | `Plugin URI:`, `Author URI:`, readme links and the account email domain must show the submitter owns any brand in the name. | Review Walkthrough › Trademarks and Project Names |
| HB-130 | "Keep in mind we do not accept 'permission' or 'authorization' at this time." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a licence/permission letter from a brand owner does not allow their name as first term. | Review Walkthrough › Trademarks and Project Names |
| HB-131 | "Plugins must be submitted under an account overtly and directly related to the representative of the project." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a branded plugin is submitted from the brand's own account/email domain. | Review Walkthrough › Trademarks and Project Names |
| HB-132 | "Check the name for typos." | Slug and `Plugin Name:` spelling. | Review Walkthrough › Typos and Long (or Short) Names |
| HB-133 | "Check the name for foul language, version numbers, and length." | Slug/`Plugin Name:`: no profanity, no version numbers (`-v2`, `2.0`), not one generic word, not an overlong chain of brand names. | Review Walkthrough › Typos and Long (or Short) Names |
| HB-134 | "we don't permit plugins to use `wordpress`, `theme`, or `plugin` in them except for extreme cases." | Slug and `Plugin Name:` containing "wordpress", "theme", "plugin" (case-insensitive). | Review Walkthrough › Restricted Terms |
| HB-135 | "If they've used `plugin` in the name and it's not a plugin that manages/handles plugins, remove the term. If they've used `wordpress` change it to `wp` and so on." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: reviewers rename the slug themselves; pre-empt by using "wp" and dropping "plugin". | Review Walkthrough › Restricted Terms |
| HB-136 | "This is only required on submission IF a plugin is a service or makes remote calls." | If any `wp_remote_*`/`fetch(`/remote asset exists, a readme.txt is mandatory at submission. | Review Walkthrough › The Readme |
| HB-137 | "If at any time in the review, it is determined the plugin requires documentation to be usable, a readme may be required even if not a service." | readme.txt present with setup instructions for any non-obvious workflow. | Review Walkthrough › The Readme |
| HB-138 | "A service must explain that it's a service, how to join it, how to register, and preferably why." | readme `== Description ==`/FAQ: "this plugin connects to <service>", sign-up URL, registration/API-key steps, reason the service is needed. | Review Walkthrough › The Readme |
| HB-139 | "If you're confused by the plugin `readme` and directions, a user will be. Push back and strive for clarity." | readme read cold by an outsider: setup steps complete, no jargon, no missing prerequisites. | Review Walkthrough › The Readme |
| HB-140 | "Open the plugin and review all the code. Line by line." | Every PHP/JS file incl. vendor and build output is read; nothing assumed safe because it is third-party. | Review Walkthrough › The Code |
| HB-141 | "`wp-(con\|blog\|load)`: Few plugins should call those terms." | grep -E `wp-(con\|blog\|load)` across the ZIP. | Review Walkthrough › The Code |
| HB-142 | "`$_(POST\|GET\|REQUEST)`: This will help you check for sanitization." | grep -E `\$_(POST\|GET\|REQUEST)`; each hit wrapped in `sanitize_*`/`absint`/`wp_unslash` and preceded by nonce + capability checks. | Review Walkthrough › The Code |
| HB-143 | "`'function '`: Notice the space there. This can help you spot badly named functions." | grep `function ` for unprefixed global function names (outside classes/namespaces). | Review Walkthrough › The Code |
| HB-144 | "Remember: trust your eyes more than the tool." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a clean Plugin Check/phpcs run (esp. with `phpcs:ignore`) proves nothing; reviewers read the code. | Review Walkthrough › The Code |
| HB-145 | "If, in your review, you determine there is no way possible for the plugin to be secure, it should be rejected." | Features that cannot be made safe: executing user-supplied code (`eval(`, `include` of user paths), arbitrary file write/read endpoints. | Review Walkthrough › Security |
| HB-146 | "This primarily happens with plugins that attempt to embed PHP… in posts." | Shortcodes/filters running post content as PHP: `eval(` inside `the_content`/`add_shortcode(` callbacks, `php://` wrappers. | Review Walkthrough › Security |
| HB-147 | "the plugins often neglect to restrict use to admins and editors, making it a huge danger on a multisite…" | Powerful actions without `current_user_can(` (and `is_super_admin()`/`unfiltered_html` on multisite); REST `permission_callback` / AJAX handlers matching the power of what they do. | Review Walkthrough › Security |
| HB-148 | "Make sure they have this at the top of the uninstall file: if( ! defined( 'WP\_UNINSTALL\_PLUGIN' ) ) exit ();" | `uninstall.php` first statement: `if ( ! defined( 'WP_UNINSTALL_PLUGIN' ) ) exit;` | Review Walkthrough › Uninstall and Deactivation |
| HB-149 | "And make sure the process is sane." | `uninstall.php` / `register_uninstall_hook(`: deletes only the plugin's own options, tables, post meta, files (prefixed); multisite loop via `get_sites()`; no deleting user media. | Review Walkthrough › Uninstall and Deactivation |
| HB-150 | "If a deactivation is used, encourage them not to delete everything on deactivation, otherwise debugging users may find their data erased." | `register_deactivation_hook(` callbacks: no `delete_option(`, `DROP TABLE`, `delete_post_meta_by_key(`, file deletion — only cron unscheduling / rewrite flush. | Review Walkthrough › Uninstall and Deactivation |
| HB-151 | "All should be unique." | Function, class, `define(` and constant names prefixed or namespaced; examples flagged: `function tags()`, `class Plugin{}`, `define( MY_PLUGIN, true)`. | Review Walkthrough › Function/Class/Define Names |
| HB-152 | "In the case of shared libraries… the developer is required to check if the library already exists and not include it if so." | Bundled libraries loaded behind `if ( ! class_exists( 'X' ) )` / `function_exists(` / `defined(` guards, or namespaced/scoped (PHP-Scoper, Strauss). | Review Walkthrough › Function/Class/Define Names |
| HB-153 | "It's important to sanitize, validate, and escape all POST/GET/REQUEST calls." | Every `$_POST`/`$_GET`/`$_REQUEST` read: validate (whitelist/`in_array`/type check), sanitize (`sanitize_text_field`, `sanitize_key`, `absint`, `esc_url_raw`), escape on output. | Review Walkthrough › Sanitization, Validation, Escaping |
| HB-154 | "Using `stripslashes` or `strip_tags` is rarely enough." | `stripslashes(`, `strip_tags(`, `htmlspecialchars(` used as the only sanitizer; replace with `wp_unslash` + `sanitize_*` / `wp_kses`. | Review Walkthrough › Sanitization, Validation, Escaping |
| HB-155 | "The ultimate goal is that invalid and unsafe data is never processed, saved, or displayed." | Data paths superglobal → option/meta/DB/file and DB → screen: check each hop has validation, sanitization, escaping. | Review Walkthrough › Sanitization, Validation, Escaping |
| HB-156 | "Examples of unsafe usage of POST data: update_option( 'my_plugin_data', $_POST['my-data'] );" | `update_option(`/`update_post_meta(`/`update_user_meta(`/`$wpdb->insert(` taking `$_POST[...]` directly. | Review Walkthrough › Sanitization, Validation, Escaping |
| HB-157 | "All actions that accept POST data should be secured with a nonce  to prevent unauthorized access." | Form handlers and `wp_ajax_*` callbacks: `check_admin_referer(` / `check_ajax_referer(` / `wp_verify_nonce(`; forms emit `wp_nonce_field(`; REST uses `X-WP-Nonce`. | Review Walkthrough › Nonces |
| HB-158 | "Remember, `check_admin_referer` alone is not bulletproof security. Do not rely on `nonces` for authorization purposes. Use `current_user_can()` in order to prevent users without the right permissions from accessing things." | Each handler with a nonce check also has `current_user_can(` for the matching capability; REST routes have a real `permission_callback` (not `__return_true` for writes). | Review Walkthrough › Nonces |
| HB-159 | "Make sure none of the default JS libraries are included in the plugin or called remotely" | Bundled or remote copies of core-shipped libraries (`ajax.googleapis.com/ajax/libs/jquery`, jquery-ui, underscore, backbone, moment, lodash, react) — use core handles. | Review Walkthrough › Javascript |
| HB-160 | "Offloading images, JS, CSS…, CGI, and other scripts to Google (or jquery.com, or anywhere else, frankly) is disallowed because it introduces an unnecessary dependency on another site." | Remote URLs in `wp_enqueue_*(`, `<img src="http`, CSS `url(http`, JS `import` from URLs. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content |
| HB-161 | "If the file is a part of WordPress Core… then it should be used, otherwise it must be included locally in the plugin, not remotely." | Assets either core handles (`wp_enqueue_script( 'jquery-ui-sortable' )`) or files inside the plugin (`plugins_url(`). | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content |
| HB-162 | "The one exception to this rule is if a plugin is performing a service. We will permit this on a case by case basis" | Remote loads only for the service's own API, disclosed in readme. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content |
| HB-163 | "Offloading jQuery CSS files to Google or a CDN. All files should be locally included." | jQuery UI themes / CSS from `ajax.googleapis.com`, `code.jquery.com`, CDNs. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › not permitted |
| HB-164 | "Inserting an iframe… with a help doc. A link, or including the docs is preferred." | `<iframe` in admin help/docs/onboarding pages pointing at the developer's site. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › not permitted |
| HB-165 | "Calling images from the author's own domain. They should be included in the plugin." | `<img src="https://<developer-domain>/…">`, CSS `url(https://…)`, remote logos/banners in admin. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › not permitted |
| HB-166 | "Calling font families from Google or their approved CDN (if GPL… compatible)." | Remote fonts OK if GPL-compatible licence (`fonts.googleapis.com`); still disclose in readme. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › permitted |
| HB-167 | "API calls back to your server to process possible spam comments (like Akismet)." | Service API calls via `wp_remote_post(` are permitted when they are the service and are disclosed. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › permitted |
| HB-168 | "Offloading comments to your own servers (like Disqus)." | Content offload to the plugin's own service — permitted as a service, disclosed in readme. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › permitted |
| HB-169 | "oEmbed calls to a service provider (like Twitter or YouTube)." | `wp_oembed_get(` / `wp_oembed_add_provider(` usage — permitted. | Review Walkthrough › Offloading Content › permitted |
| HB-170 | "Plugins are permitted to save files, however they cannot save them to the `plugins` folder, as that gets deleted on plugin upgrade." | File writes targeting `WP_PLUGIN_DIR`, `plugin_dir_path(`, `__DIR__`. | Review Walkthrough › Saving Files |
| HB-171 | "We recommend files be saved to `wp-content/uploads/pluginname` as that can easily be made multisite compatible (via `wp_upload_dir`) and it will always be writable." | Writes built from `wp_upload_dir()['basedir']` + plugin subfolder; no hard-coded `wp-content/uploads`. | Review Walkthrough › Saving Files |
| HB-172 | "Saving 'temp' files to a plugin folder can be permitted, however make sure they're deleted properly." | Temp writes use `get_temp_dir()`/`wp_tempnam(` or are `unlink(`ed after use (incl. on error paths). | Review Walkthrough › Saving Files |
| HB-173 | "we permit the use of 3rd party libraries, however they must be the latest stable version." | `vendor/` + `composer.lock` / `package.json` / library file headers: versions = latest stable release. | Review Walkthrough › 3rd Party Libraries |
| HB-174 | "The library also must be actively maintained, as the unmaintained are prone to security issues." | Each bundled library has recent releases/commits; abandoned packages replaced. | Review Walkthrough › 3rd Party Libraries |
| HB-175 | "Examples of unmaintained libraries include: TimThumb… MVC2… CSS Tidy… Titan Framework… Magnificent Popup" | grep `timthumb`, `csstidy`, `class.csstidy`, `titan-framework`, `TitanFramework`, `magnific-popup`, `MVC2` in the ZIP. | Review Walkthrough › 3rd Party Libraries |
| HB-176 | "In those cases, we permit the most recent versions, however we encourage developers to update as older versions that do not receive security updates will not be permitted." | Bootstrap and similar hard-to-update libraries: shipped major still receiving security updates. | Review Walkthrough › 3rd Party Libraries |

## hb-performing-reviews_security-and-guideline-violation-reports.txt — Security and Guideline Violation Reports
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/performing-reviews/security-and-guideline-violation-reports/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-177 | "Each one must be read, reviewed, and replied to without exception." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: any report against the plugin (by users, firms, competitors) will be examined. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports (intro) |
| HB-178 | "all plugins in the WordPress.org Plugin Directory  are required to be compatible with the GPLv2 or later" | Plugin header `License:`, readme `License:`/`License URI:`, licences of all bundled code. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports (intro) |
| HB-179 | "All reports must be checked by a human being to ensure they are valid. If someone reports a vulnerability it must be tested." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: reported vulnerabilities are reproduced; keep endpoints' capability checks provable. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Verification |
| HB-180 | "we no longer permit plugins to use someone else's trademark/product name as their plugin slug, or the first term(s) of their plugin." | Slug and first words of `Plugin Name:`/readme title vs. other products' names. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Copyright/Trademark Violations |
| HB-181 | "all plugins submitted prior to 2015 are 'grandfathered' in, and we will not close them unless there is a pressing need to do so." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: older plugins with brand-first names are no precedent for a new submission. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Copyright/Trademark Violations |
| HB-182 | "All non-official plugins with names that no longer are approved should state in their name and/or description that they are not the official plugin." | readme short description / `== Description ==`: "not affiliated with / not the official <Product> plugin" where a third-party product is named. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Copyright/Trademark Violations |
| HB-183 | "Every attempt is made to ensure only the authorized user of a name can use a name. If a mistake is made and it is critical enough, plugins may be closed." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: an approved name that infringes can still be closed later. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Copyright/Trademark Violations |
| HB-184 | "If you can't reproduce it, reply to the reporter and tell them so." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: unreproducible reports do not close a plugin. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Security Reports |
| HB-185 | "if the issue is not the plugin but a decision made in WordPress, we do not close the plugin. For example, administrators can post JavaScript… to post titles." | Behaviour gated on core capabilities (`unfiltered_html`) is core's decision; anything a lower role can do beyond core's rules is the plugin's bug. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Security Reports |
| HB-186 | "Everyone who officially represents a plugin is required to abide by the guidelines." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: staff, contractors and agencies acting for the plugin bind the plugin. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Behavioral Violations |
| HB-187 | "If someone working for a plugin's company makes sock puppets, report it to the plugin owner with a stern warning." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: no fake reviews/forum accounts by anyone connected to the plugin. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Behavioral Violations |
| HB-188 | "If, following a warning, a developer escalates the behavior, all their plugins will be closed." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: after a warning, any repeat closes every plugin of the account. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Behavioral Violations |
| HB-189 | "Abusing moderators and reviewers is not permitted." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: tone in review replies and forums. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Behavioral Violations |
| HB-190 | "Under no circumstances should you divulge exactly how the fake accounts were spotted." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: no detail will be given on sockpuppet detection; don't ask. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Sockpuppets |
| HB-191 | "Rewarded users for leaving reviews is not permitted. Period." | `admin_notices`/review-request prompts offering credits, discounts, coupons, Pro features or entries for a review (`wordpress.org/support/plugin/<slug>/reviews` links near "free", "credits", "discount", "coupon"). | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review Compensation |
| HB-192 | "Developers cannot pay, offer discounts, or otherwise 'give back' to users who leave reviews." | Same as above, plus emails/site copy outside the plugin offering rewards for reviews. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review Compensation |
| HB-193 | "all reviews from the time the offer was made until it was removed will be archived in the WordPress forums. They will not be restored" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: one review-incentive offer costs every review from that period, permanently. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review Compensation |
| HB-194 | "With few exceptions, plugins are closed immediately when a security issue is confirmed." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a confirmed vulnerability closes the plugin with no grace period; ship capability/nonce/escaping fixes proactively. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Close the Plugin |
| HB-195 | "Therefore the plugin is closed and the onus is on the developer to fix the plugin." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: reopening is driven by the developer's fix and reply. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Close the Plugin |
| HB-196 | "If the plugin developer has notably argued and been reluctant to fix issues, DO close the plugin." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: arguing with a security finding makes closure more likely; fix first. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Close the Plugin |
| HB-197 | "Closed plugins push no updates." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: while closed, existing users get no fix through wordpress.org. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Disable vs Close |
| HB-198 | "Did they fix the change in SVN…?" | Fix committed to SVN `trunk` and the tagged release (`tags/<version>`), not only on GitHub. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review the Fix |
| HB-199 | "Did they increment their version number?" | Plugin header `Version:`, readme `Stable tag:`, any version constant (`define( 'PREFIX_VERSION'`) all bumped and equal; new SVN tag exists. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review the Fix |
| HB-200 | "Are there any other guideline violations or security issues you noted?" | Full re-audit, not only the reported issue. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review the Fix |
| HB-201 | "Would they pass a review today?" | Run the current Review Checklist rows (this file) against the fixed release. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review the Fix |
| HB-202 | "Remember: All changes must be tracked in SVN, the version number must be increased, and the plugin must pass a review 'today'." | SVN log shows the fix commit; version bump; full checklist pass. | Security and Guideline Violation Reports › Review the Fix |

## hb-directory-maintenance.txt — Directory Maintenance
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/directory-maintenance/

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| HB-203 | "There are some filters on SVN… that prevent dangerous code from being committed to the repositories, the rest of the work is done by scanning." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: an SVN commit can be refused by pre-commit filters, and every commit is later scanned repo-wide. | Directory Maintenance (intro) |
| HB-204 | "Any time a plugin is closed, the developers must be contacted, and the action must be logged on the private P2" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a closure always comes with an email to the owner. | Directory Maintenance › Removing Plugins |
| HB-205 | "Security issues" | Immediate closure trigger: capability/nonce gaps, unescaped output, SQL without `prepare(`, file-upload/write endpoints. | Directory Maintenance › Immediate Closure |
| HB-206 | "GPL… violations" | Immediate closure trigger: non-GPL-compatible bundled code, licence keys gating code shipped in the free plugin. | Directory Maintenance › Immediate Closure |
| HB-207 | "Flagrant abuse of other community members" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: immediate closure for abusive conduct. | Directory Maintenance › Immediate Closure |
| HB-208 | "Users being banned from the repository (all plugins are closed when this happens)" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: a ban closes every plugin on the account. | Directory Maintenance › Immediate Closure |
| HB-209 | "Intentionally disguising themselves to get around previous blocks/bans" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: alternate accounts after a block mean immediate closure. | Directory Maintenance › Immediate Closure |
| HB-210 | "Except in extreme cases, make sure the developer(s) has received at least one warning before closing plugins." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: for the warn-first offences below, a warning email precedes closure; act on it. | Directory Maintenance › Immediate Closure |
| HB-211 | "Sockpuppets (delete all suspect reviews first)" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: no reviews from staff/friends' accounts. | Directory Maintenance › Reasons to warn first |
| HB-212 | "Self promotion in related plugin support threads or reviews" | Process — not in code; implication for developer: don't advertise your plugin in other plugins' forums/reviews (e.g. in a competitor's support threads). | Directory Maintenance › Reasons to warn first |
| HB-213 | "Tag… abuse" | readme `Tags:` line: few, accurate tags; no competitor/brand names or keyword stuffing. | Directory Maintenance › Reasons to warn first |
| HB-214 | "README scamming" | readme content: no keyword stuffing, fake feature claims, competitor names for search, hidden text. | Directory Maintenance › Reasons to warn first |
| HB-215 | "Frequent commits to game the 'recently updated' list" | SVN history: no trivial/no-op commits or tag bumps (e.g. only `Tested up to:`/whitespace changes in rapid succession). | Directory Maintenance › Reasons to warn first |
| HB-216 | "If a warning has been sent with no response, send a second warning or "FINAL WARNING" and explain the behavior must stop. Provide them with a realistic due date." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: warnings carry deadlines (e.g. 4–5 business days); missing one leads to closure. | Directory Maintenance › Reasons to warn first |
| HB-217 | "Verify the request came from either a developer with commit access or someone who works for the company." | Process — not in code; implication for developer: removal requests must come from the owner's committer email/company. | Directory Maintenance › By Request |
| HB-218 | "Looking for `global $tag` in all PHP… files" | grep `global[^;]+\$tag` — globals with generic names like `$tag` (unprefixed globals collide with core). | Directory Maintenance › Examples |
| HB-219 | "Find everyone using HTTP… in an enqueue" | grep `wp_enqueue_(style\|script)\s*\(.*["']http://` — enqueues over plain HTTP (and remote enqueues generally). | Directory Maintenance › Examples |
| HB-220 | "Find all instances of a specific file:" | Reviewers find bundled copies of known files repo-wide (example `*datepicker-*.js`): check for bundled copies of core/known-vulnerable library files by filename. | Directory Maintenance › Examples |

## hb-glossary.txt — Glossary
URL: not stated in the saved file; canonical page https://make.wordpress.org/plugins/handbook/glossary/

No rules stated; 0 rows. (Definitions only: Admin P2, FUD, Help Scout, Phone home, RTFM, Sockpuppet, SupportPress, SVN. "Phone home" — "sends back tracking information to the plugin developer once it’s installed on a site. This may include IP addresses, usernames, or other data." — defines the term used by the checklist's "Don't phone home" row; it imposes nothing by itself.)

## post-a-year-in-the-plugins-team-2025.txt — A Year in the Plugins Team – 2025 (David Perez, 2026-01-07)
URL: not stated in the saved file; make.wordpress.org/plugins post

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST-1 | "Verifying that the plugin name does not conflict with existing published plugins." | `Plugin Name:` header and readme `=== Name ===` line; search wordpress.org/plugins for the same or near-identical name | Year 2025 |
| POST-2 | "Ensuring branding is used correctly and complies with guidelines." | Third-party trademarks/brands in `Plugin Name:`, slug, readme title, banner/icon assets | Year 2025 |
| POST-3 | "Verifying plugin ownership." | Process — check: submitting wordpress.org account matches `Author:` / `Author URI:` / `Plugin URI:` brand and domain | Year 2025 |
| POST-4 | "New security checks: 5+ (nonce verification, direct DB queries, forbidden functions, minified files, wp_safe_redirect)" | `wp_verify_nonce(` / `check_admin_referer(` / `check_ajax_referer(` on every form/AJAX/REST write; `$wpdb->query(` / `get_results(` / `get_var(` without `$wpdb->prepare`; forbidden functions such as `eval(`, `create_function(`; `*.min.js` / `*.min.css` without shipped source; `wp_redirect(` where `wp_safe_redirect(` is required | Year 2025 |
| POST-5 | "New code quality checks: 10+ (prefixing, textdomain improvements, localhost detection, etc.)" | Unprefixed `function `, `class `, `define(`, `add_option(`, hook names; `Text Domain:` equals slug and matches every `__(`/`_e(`/`esc_html__(` domain; `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` | Year 2025 |
| POST-6 | "Expanded license support: added ISC, MPL-2.0, and The Unlicense" | `License:` / `License URI:` in plugin header and readme; bundled libraries' licences are GPL-compatible | Year 2025 |
| POST-7 | "we have implemented PCP to run automatic scans on every new plugin version update ." | Process — check: run `wp plugin check <slug>` on every release before tagging, not only on first submission | Year 2025 |
| POST-8 | "We continue to recommend that authors follow best practices such as the WordPress Coding Standards … and set up automated workflows—such as GitHub Actions —to have their plugins reviewed by Plugin Check as part of their development process." | Process — check: PHPCS with `WordPress` ruleset passes; a CI job runs the Plugin Check GitHub Action | Year 2025 |
| POST-9 | "AI has lowered the barriers to entry without compromising plugin quality (since the “barrier” for plugin approval has not been lowered)." | Process — check: AI-written code gets the same escaping/sanitising/nonce/prefix review as hand-written code | Year 2025 |
| POST-10 | "This situation continues to challenge the team to keep the queue for a first review under one week" | Process — check: plan for ~1 week to first review | Year 2025 |
| POST-11 | "Highlighting once again that active developer engagement strongly correlates with successful approval." | Process — check: reply to every review email (38.7% of reviewed plugins got no author reply) | Year 2025 |

## post-automated-security-review-for-plugin-releases.txt — Automated security review for plugin releases (David Perez, 2026-09-09)
URL: not stated in the saved file; make.wordpress.org/plugins post

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST-12 | "Before a release is distributed through the WordPress.org… update API…, it is analyzed for security issues and releases that are considered to pose a potential security risk (either intentional or unintentional)." | Process — check: every release diff is scanned; before tagging, review the diff for `eval(`, `base64_decode(`, `system(`/`exec(`/`shell_exec(`, unescaped output, unprepared SQL, missing `current_user_can(` / nonce | Automated security review |
| POST-13 | "every plugin and theme release goes through a cooldown before it is distributed through the WordPress.org update API, including one-click updates from the WordPress dashboard. The cooldown period is currently at 6 hours." | Process — check: a tagged release reaches sites no sooner than ~6 hours after commit; plan hotfixes accordingly | Automated security review |
| POST-14 | "During the cooldown, the changes in each release are analyzed in WordPress.org by several AI models together with Jetpack Scan." | Process — check: the diff between the previous and new `Stable tag` is what is scanned; keep release diffs small and readable | Automated security review |
| POST-15 | "Releases with a high risk score are blocked automatically as soon as the security review finishes, and all plugin committers receive an email with the findings." | Process — check: all committers' wordpress.org emails are monitored after each release | Automated security review |
| POST-16 | "A high score does not mean malicious intent. An accidentally introduced vulnerability can score just as high as intentional malware. The score measures risk, not intent." | Same security greps as POST-12 on new code paths (REST `permission_callback`, AJAX handlers, file writes, `$wpdb->prepare`) | Automated security review |
| POST-17 | "A high risk score means the release will not be distributed through the update API until the issues are resolved." | Process — check: a blocked release stays undistributed; do not assume a tag reached users | Automated security review |
| POST-18 | "Review the reported findings. They describe what triggered the block…." | Process — check: read the findings email before changing code | Automated security review |
| POST-19 | "Fix the issues and publish a new release. When the new release scores below the blocking threshold, it continues through the normal cooldown process." | Process — check: fix ships as a new version (bump `Version:` and `Stable tag:`), not a re-tag | Automated security review |
| POST-20 | "If a finding looks incorrect, authors can contact the Plugins Team." | Process — check: false-positive appeal goes to the Plugins Team | Automated security review |
| POST-21 | "publishing a fixed release is almost always faster than waiting for a manual review of an appeal." | Process — check: prefer a fixed release over an appeal | Automated security review |
| POST-22 | "Right now, emails are only sent when a release is blocked. Authors who haven’t received an email don’t need to take any action." | Process — check: no email = release passed | Automated security review |
| POST-23 | "Feedback about false positives is the most useful thing plugin authors can give us to make the system better." | Process — check: report false positives | Automated security review |

## post-guidance-on-plugins-that-install-other-plugins.txt — Guidance on plugins that install other plugins (Francisco Torres, 2024-09-09)
URL: not stated in the saved file; make.wordpress.org/plugins post

| ID | Rule (quoted from the source; shorten only by cutting, never paraphrase) | What to grep/read for in a PHP/JS WordPress plugin or its readme | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST-24 | "The current guidelines have different indications regarding the context in which other plugins are installed. For example: no tracking without consent , guidelines regarding executable code , dismissible notices , trialware , etc." | Install/onboarding flows: `wp_remote_post(` / `wp_remote_get(` telemetry without opt-in; admin notices recommending plugins carry `is-dismissible` and stay dismissed; no features locked behind installing another plugin | Install other plugins |
| POST-25 | "Users must be adequately informed of the actions they are taking and be able to decide whether they want to perform that action or not, otherwise that would be considered dishonest towards the users ." | Every code path reaching `Plugin_Upgrader`, `->install(`, `wp.updates.installPlugin`, `activate_plugin(` starts from an explicit user click with the action named on the button | Install other plugins |
| POST-26 | "mentions this specific case which won’t be allowed: “Serving updates or otherwise installing plugins, themes, or add-ons from servers other than WordPress.org…’s”." | `download_url(`, `unzip_file(`, `Plugin_Upgrader` / `Theme_Upgrader` `->install(` with a non-`downloads.wordpress.org` URL; `pre_set_site_transient_update_plugins`, `site_transient_update_plugins`, `plugins_api` filters pointing at own server; `wp_remote_get(` fetching zips | Install other plugins |
| POST-27 | "This simply means that a plugin will not be able to perform the installation of another plugin that is not in the directory." | Any installer target slug/URL: must be a wordpress.org directory plugin (e.g. via `plugins_api( 'plugin_information'` ), never a Pro/add-on zip | Install other plugins |
| POST-28 | "The way to install plugins that are not part of the directory will be a manual installation by the user." | Pro/add-on upsell: link to download page + manual upload instructions only; no in-plugin download/install of the Pro zip | Install other plugins |
| POST-29 | "In this case, the installation of that other plugin is a requirement, since the plugin won’t be able to work without it." | Extended-plugin dependency (e.g. WooCommerce add-on): may be stated as required only when the plugin cannot work without it | Install other plugins |
| POST-30 | "the WordPress core now includes support for required plugins . If you have a plugin that extends another plugin in the directory, we recommend that you use it." | Plugin header `Requires Plugins:` (directory slugs) instead of custom dependency installers | Install other plugins |
| POST-31 | "while there are different reasons behind it, they all fall into the same category…: a plugin recommendation and their installation should be optional." | Recommended / cooperating / ecosystem / Pro plugins: no gating, no "required" wording, install path skippable | Install other plugins |
| POST-32 | "Then the plugin must be manually installed by the user, either using the search plugins feature (if the plugin is in the directory), uploading a zip file, or uploading it to the /plugins/ directory." | Manual path: link to `plugin-install.php?s=…&tab=search` or instructions; no programmatic install | Install other plugins |
| POST-33 | "instead of asking the user to manually install it, it uses the interface that the WordPress core already provides to install it." | Core UX: `plugin-install.php?tab=plugin-information&plugin=<slug>` in `thickbox`, `install-now` button class, `wp.updates.installPlugin` | Install other plugins |
| POST-34 | "They can see the plugin’s name, description, version, etc. and the call to action is a clear button with the text “Install Now”." | Install UI shows name, description, version; button text is an explicit install action | Install other plugins |
| POST-35 | "In this interface it’s important to get the user’s consent after providing them with sufficient information about what’s being installed." | Custom UX (options page, setup wizard, onboarding): plugin named and described before the consent click; read wizard JS/PHP for silent `Plugin_Upgrader` calls | Install other plugins |
| POST-36 | "Automatically install plugins without informing the user and/or asking for their permission. This is expressly not allowed." | `Plugin_Upgrader` / `->install(` / `activate_plugin(` on `register_activation_hook(`, `admin_init`, `init`, `upgrader_process_complete`, cron, or REST without a user click | Install other plugins |
| POST-37 | "Why install other plugins?Extended pluginsRecommended plugins … As a requirement✅❌" | Only an extended (dependency) plugin may be required; a recommended plugin must never be presented as required | Install other plugins |
| POST-38 | "OptionallyN/A✅" | Recommended plugins: install offered as optional only | Install other plugins |
| POST-39 | "How are other plugins installed?ManuallyCore UXCustom UXNo-asking … Plugins in the directory✅✅✅❌" | Directory plugins: manual, Core UX or Custom UX allowed; no-asking install forbidden | Install other plugins |
| POST-40 | "External plugins✅❌❌❌" | Non-directory plugins: manual install only; no Core UX, Custom UX or automatic install of external zips | Install other plugins |
| POST-41 | "while the general rule is “get the user’s consent after providing them with sufficient information about what’s being installed”, we recognize that this is on a case-by-case basis, and is somewhat subjective." | Custom install UI judged case by case: read the screen as a user and confirm consent is informed | Install other plugins |
| POST-42 | "users have all the information they need to make a decision about installing a plugin, and a clear and easy way to give their consent." | Install UI: plugin info visible plus a clear consent control | Install other plugins |
| POST-43 | "One suggestion would be to make the information about installing a plugin the most prominent information in the area where the user chooses to install it." | (proposal) Install notice is the most prominent text, not small print beside the option | Install other plugins |
| POST-44 | "A suggestion would be to make that option not selected by default, so that the user has to take explicit action regarding that particular plugin in order to install it." | (proposal) No pre-checked install checkboxes: grep `checked` / `checked="checked"` / `defaultChecked` / `:checked="true"` on install options in wizards | Install other plugins |
| POST-45 | "One suggestion would be to require plugins to be installed one at a time in a process that requires explicit user action to install them, by clicking a button that clearly states what it does." | (proposal) No loop installing several plugins from one click (`foreach` around `->install(` / `installPlugin`) | Install other plugins |
| POST-46 | "One suggestion is to provide access to all information about the plugin, and perhaps the easiest way to do that would be to clearly link to the WordPress.org plugin page for that plugin." | (proposal) Install UI links `https://wordpress.org/plugins/<slug>/` | Install other plugins |
| POST-47 | "The suggestion in this case would be to route any plugin installation process through this interface." | (proposal) Use core `plugin-install.php` / `wp.updates.installPlugin` rather than a custom installer | Install other plugins |
| POST-48 | "After that, there will be a 3-month period during which plugin authors will be able to make the necessary changes" | Process — check: a final decision post would give 3 months to comply | Install other plugins |

Not stated in this saved file: the `install_plugins` capability, auto-activation, TGMPA, or `Plugin_Upgrader` by name. The rows use those only as grep targets.
